## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-1** · P2 — `analytics.scoring_watermarks` enables RLS but is not FORCE-enabled and has no policies
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260827070000_analytics_scoring_watermarks.sql:24
    - **Affects:** `analytics.scoring_watermarks`; defense-in-depth against direct table access outside the `app_backend` path (PostgREST / Supabase client).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - `ALTER TABLE "analytics"."scoring_watermarks" FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;`
        - Add an explicit `app_backend` `FOR ALL` policy (this table has no tenant column — it's a singleton ops row, `id = 'popularity'` — so the policy is simply "role = app_backend", not a tenant-scoped predicate).
        - Add a `tests/Schema/` introspection guard for this table, following `ModerationSchemaRlsTest`'s pattern.
    - **Technical:** The migration only runs `ALTER TABLE ... ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;`. Without `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, the table owner role bypasses RLS silently; without any `CREATE POLICY`, no role other than the owner or a `BYPASSRLS` role (`app_backend`) can read or write it at all. The house pattern for every new schema table — established in `20260602000000_design_kits_rls.sql` and `ModerationSchemaRlsTest` — is RLS enabled + FORCE + explicit per-role policies, and this table skipped the last two steps.
    - **Plain English:** The new scoring ledger has a security lock installed but not switched on, and no one has been given a key. Today the one privileged service account walks around the lock entirely, so nothing looks broken — but that's exactly the situation defense-in-depth is meant to catch before it matters.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE "analytics"."scoring_watermarks" ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-2** · P2 — `content.offers.platform` is a free-form text column with no CHECK constraint despite a documented finite vocabulary
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260826200000_offers_per_item_platform_links.sql:36-39
    - **Affects:** Ordering-platform attribution integrity for `content.offers`; any reader that switches on the platform slug (`ManualMenuItems::offerBelongsTo`, `ItemLinkRules::platformForUrl` callers).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `ADD CONSTRAINT offers_platform_check CHECK (platform IN ('square', 'uber-eats', 'doordash')) NOT VALID;` then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in a separate transaction (table is currently small, but split anyway to match convention).
        - Mirror the vocabulary in `MenuProjectionMapper` if not already centralized there.
    - **Technical:** The migration's own header comment documents `platform` as a closed slug vocabulary (`'square' | 'uber-eats' | 'doordash'`) but declares the column as nullable `text` with no DB constraint. The value is currently only written by `MenuProjectionMapper` (internal, not user input), which limits exposure today, but nothing stops a future writer or a hand-run repair script from persisting a typo'd or drifted value that switch-style readers silently miss. Canonical pattern: `site.sites.architecture_id CHECK (architecture_id = 'one')`.
    - **Plain English:** You've told the system there are only three approved ordering apps, but the database itself will accept any text in that field. It's like a drop-down that quietly accepts free typing — a future bug could write "doordesh" and nothing would complain until a customer's order silently fails to route.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE "content"."offers"
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "platform" text NULL,
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "item_url" text NULL,
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "external_ref" text NULL;
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-3** · P2 — New `ON DELETE CASCADE` FKs on four `content.*` tables have no index leading with `source_item_id`, so cascade deletes fall back to sequential scans
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260826120000_facet_source_item_origin.sql:34-48; companion index files supabase/migrations/20260826120001-04_*.sql
    - **Affects:** Cascading deletes from `content.source_items` into `content.item_media`, `content.offers`, `content.item_tags`, `content.item_variants` — fired today by `AccountDeletionService`'s user-deletion cascade (`core.users` → `content.items`/`content.sources` → `content.source_items` → these four tables).
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add one `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS ... ON content.<table> (source_item_id)` per child table, each alone in its own migration file (per `CONVENTIONS.md` §1 — `CONCURRENTLY` can't share a file).
        - Keep the existing `(item_id, source_item_id)` indexes — they serve a different, real query path (`replaceCollections()`'s origin-scoped delete) and are not redundant with the new ones.
    - **Technical:** Each new FK is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a `content.source_items` row makes Postgres scan each child table for `WHERE source_item_id = $1`. The only indexes on these four tables lead with `item_id`, e.g. `("item_id", "source_item_id")` — a predicate on `source_item_id` alone can't use that composite index selectively and falls back to a much less efficient scan. This is reachable today via `AccountDeletionService`'s real cascade chain, not a hypothetical.
    - **Plain English:** When someone's account is deleted and a source item is removed with it, the database has to find every related record that traces back to it. Right now those records are only filed under "item, then source" — so looking things up by source alone means checking every single filing card instead of jumping straight to the right ones. A dedicated source-only index fixes that.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE "content"."item_media"
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "source_item_id" uuid NULL
            REFERENCES "content"."source_items" ("id") ON DELETE CASCADE;
        ```
        ```sql
        CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS "idx_item_media_origin" ON "content"."item_media" ("item_id", "source_item_id");
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-4** · P2 — Four full-table backfill `UPDATE`s run in the same file as their DDL, contradicting the repo's own "never backfill inside a migration" convention
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260826120000_facet_source_item_origin.sql:54-112
    - **Affects:** `content.item_media`, `content.offers`, `content.item_tags`, `content.item_variants`; deploy latency as these tables grow (currently small, pre-pilot).
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Split the four `UPDATE` backfills into a separate migration file (or a post-deploy artisan command), per `CONVENTIONS.md` §5's explicit "GOOD" pattern.
        - These four tables are not on the `HOT_TABLES` list (`site.design_kits`, `site.sites`, `site.blocks`, `core.users`) so `SET LOCAL lock_timeout`/`statement_timeout` isn't required by convention today — but splitting the backfill out still matters once the tables grow.
    - **Technical:** The file has more than one statement, so per `CONVENTIONS.md` §1 the Supabase CLI applier sends the whole file — all four `ADD COLUMN`s and all four `UPDATE...FROM...NOT EXISTS` backfills — as a single libpq pipeline (an implicit transaction). `CONVENTIONS.md` §5 explicitly calls this pattern out as unsafe ("Never backfill data inside a migration transaction... BAD — migration holds ACCESS EXCLUSIVE while updating millions of rows") and shows the two-file split as the fix. The tables are small today (the migration's own comment describes a narrow, unambiguous backfill), so there's no current incident risk, but the pattern will bite as `content.*` grows post-launch.
    - **Plain English:** This migration asks the database to repaint four rooms as part of installing new fixtures, all in one uninterruptible visit. Today the rooms are small so nobody notices, but the house's own rulebook says repainting should always be a separate, schedulable job — not bundled into the fixture install — precisely so it doesn't become a problem once the rooms get bigger.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        UPDATE "content"."item_media" m
        SET "source_item_id" = si."id"
        FROM "content"."source_items" si
        WHERE si."item_id" = m."item_id"
          AND si."source_id" = m."source_id"
          AND si."removed_at" IS NULL
          AND m."source_item_id" IS NULL
          AND NOT EXISTS (
              SELECT 1 FROM "content"."source_items" si2
              WHERE si2."item_id" = m."item_id"
                AND si2."source_id" = m."source_id"
                AND si2."removed_at" IS NULL
                AND si2."id" <> si."id"
          );
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-5** · P2 — `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` runs in the same transaction as its `ADD CONSTRAINT ... NOT VALID`, defeating the split, and violates the repo's own automated guard (Check 8)
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260827072000_event_item_family.sql:17-49
    - **Affects:** `analytics.content_popularity_scores`, `analytics.item_views` — write-heavy analytics ingest tables.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Split into two files/transactions: `DROP`/`ADD CONSTRAINT ... NOT VALID` with its own `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`, then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in a second file with its own `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`.
        - Re-run `scripts/guard-no-unsafe-migrations.php` locally — Check 8 (added for files timestamped after `20260722000000`) is written to catch exactly this pattern (`ADD CONSTRAINT ... NOT VALID` followed by `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` with no `COMMIT` between them), and this file's timestamp (`20260827072000`) falls inside its enforcement window.
    - **Technical:** The migration correctly uses `NOT VALID`, but bundles the `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` into the same `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` block, holding the DDL's heavier lock through the whole validation scan — exactly the anti-pattern `CONVENTIONS.md` §2 and Check 8 exist to prevent. The migration's own comment argues it's safe "here" because "both tables are small on the dev ref" — true today, but `content_popularity_scores`/`item_views` are analytics-ingest tables that will grow, and prod schema reconciliation is still pending (prod currently lacks these tables/migrations entirely per `CLAUDE.md`). Whoever eventually replays this against a populated table inherits a full-table scan under `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` instead of the intended `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE`.
    - **Plain English:** The safety latch that's supposed to let the database keep serving traffic while it double-checks old data was installed and then immediately triggered in the same breath — which cancels out the point of having it. It's harmless while the table is nearly empty, but the same migration will replay verbatim against a bigger, busier table later, and at that point it will lock out writes for as long as the check takes.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE analytics.content_popularity_scores
            ADD CONSTRAINT content_popularity_scores_content_type_check
            CHECK (content_type IN (
                'action', 'shop_product', 'menu_item', 'menu_category',
                'service', 'service_category', 'block', 'gallery_item', 'engine_item',
                'listen_item', 'watch_item', 'link_item', 'event_item'
            )) NOT VALID;
        ...
        ALTER TABLE analytics.content_popularity_scores
            VALIDATE CONSTRAINT content_popularity_scores_content_type_check;

        ALTER TABLE analytics.item_views
            VALIDATE CONSTRAINT item_views_item_type_check;
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-SCHEMA-6** · P3 — `content.items.eligible_cache` dropped directly rather than via a rename-to-deprecated cycle
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260827071000_drop_items_eligible_cache.sql:10
    - **Affects:** `content.items`; general migration-hygiene consistency.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** No action needed beyond noting the deviation — verified below that it's safe in this instance. Apply the rename-first cycle for future column drops where the writer isn't already fully decoupled.
    - **Technical:** `CONVENTIONS.md`/lens doctrine call for renaming a column to `_deprecated` before a physical drop, to survive a rolling deploy where old code might still read or write it. Here that risk doesn't materialize: `grep` across `app/` confirms zero remaining references to `eligible_cache` (only a historical docblock note in `DataExportPayloadBuilder.php`), and the writer removal shipped in the same plan step (`1a2201362`, "eligible_cache dropped (plan 01 steps 6+7)") as this migration — so there's no in-flight code path that could still write to the now-missing column. Flagging for the record since it's a real deviation from the documented pattern, not because it caused (or will cause) an incident.
    - **Plain English:** Best practice says "put a retired sticker on the old drawer for one release before removing it," and this skipped straight to removal. In this specific case nothing was still using the drawer — the team had already stopped referencing it in the same batch of changes — so no harm was done, but it's worth keeping the habit for next time in case the timing isn't as clean.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE "content"."items" DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS "eligible_cache";
        ```

    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260827080000_drop_design_kit_vestige_columns.sql:32-35

The code removal shipped in the same plan step (`4bba567b7`) as this migration, so the rolling-deploy hazard the doctrine guards against didn't apply here.

Because the team had already stopped using these three settings everywhere else in the same change, nothing broke — but it's a pattern worth tightening up for future drops that aren't as tightly coordinated.

        ```sql

            DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS "border_thickness",
        ```

# Caching: Gold-Standard Adherence Audit — 2026-08-27

**Branch:** development
**Lens:** Caching: gold-standard adherence audit — deviations from `CacheLockService`/`SiteCacheService`/`UserCacheService` gold standard across single-flight locking, TTL jitter, SWR, push-invalidation, version tokens, lock hygiene, TTL bounds, and key-generation centralisation.
**Pipeline:** scan-tier draft by `deepseek-v4-pro`, adjudicated by `claude-opus-4.6`
**Source files audited:**
- app/Routing/LinkProjector.php
- app/Http/Controllers/Api/Staff/UserSiteManagement/StaffUserController.php

## Progress

- P0 Blockers: 0 of 0 complete
- P1 High: 2 of 2 complete

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W1-CCH-1** · P1 — Workplace identity-mirror write invalidates the site cache before the mirrored user row is written, leaving a stale-cache race for non-contact fields
    - **Where:** app/Observers/Core/WorkplaceObserver.php:67-82
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage readers for business accounts with `workplace_brand_is_site_identity` — a concurrent visitor can be served a rebuilt cache entry carrying the pre-edit bio/location, which then persists for the full TTL because the subsequent user save doesn't always re-bust the site cache.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Move `mirrorIdentityFields($workplace)` to run before the `SiteCacheInvalidator::touchSite(...)` call in `saved()`, so the site cache is invalidated only after both the workplace row and the mirrored user row are committed.
        - Alternatively, call `touchSite(...)` a second time after `mirrorIdentityFields()` completes.
    - **Technical:** Category 9 — cache-then-database race. `WorkplaceObserver::saved()` (afterCommit, so this runs after the *workplace* transaction commits) invalidates the site cache via `touchSite()` first, then separately writes mirrored identity fields onto `core.users` via `mirrorIdentityFields()` → `$user->save()`. Between those two steps, a concurrent public-payload read can rebuild-and-cache the value using the *pre-mirror* `User` row. Critically, `UserObserver::updated()`'s `touchParentSiteIfPublicFieldChanged()` only re-busts the site cache when one of `PUBLIC_PROFILE_USER_FIELDS` (`handle`, `display_name`, `first_name`, `last_name`, `public_contact_number`, `public_contact_email`) changed. `IDENTITY_MIRROR` also writes `bio`, `location_street_address`, `location_city`, `location_state`, `location_postcode`, and `location_country` — none of which are in that list — so when only those fields change, the second `$user->save()` does **not** re-invalidate the site cache, and the stale value populated during the race window survives for the full site-payload TTL rather than self-healing.
    - **Plain English:** When a business owner edits their address or bio, the system clears the cached version of their public page, then updates the actual address/bio record a beat later. If a visitor loads the page in that gap, they get a freshly-baked page — but baked with the *old* address, and because updating the address doesn't trigger a second cache-clear, that wrong page can stick around until the cache naturally expires instead of fixing itself right away.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($workplace->wasRecentlyCreated || $workplace->wasChanged(self::CACHE_AFFECTING_COLUMNS)) {
            $this->invalidator->touchSite(
                fn () => $workplace->site,
                'workplace-save',
                ['site_id' => $workplace->site_id],
            );
        }
        ```
        ```php
        if ($workplace->wasRecentlyCreated || $workplace->wasChanged(array_keys(self::IDENTITY_MIRROR))) {
            $this->mirrorIdentityFields($workplace);
        }
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): FIXED, with two corrections to the finding's technical block. (1) It is a wrong-line ordering bug, not a transaction-boundary bug — WorkplaceObserver already has $afterCommit = true. (2) The second $user->save() DOES fire lane 1 (UserObserver passes bustSite: true -> invalidateSitePayload DELs site:payload:{subdomain}); what it did not do is advance site.sites.updated_at, so the public.profile:{handle}:{ts} entry — a different key, never busted by design because rotation IS the design — survived. Exposure is the 60s primary TTL plus its x10 stale twin (~10 min), not the full payload TTL. Blast radius is narrower than stated: of the eight IDENTITY_MIRROR columns, phone/contact_email are already in PUBLIC_PROFILE_USER_FIELDS (self-healing) and location_* reach no public surface; only description -> users.bio was exposed. Fixed by moving the mirror above the touchSite block AND adding bio to PUBLIC_PROFILE_USER_FIELDS so every bio writer rotates the key, not just this one.

- [x] **#W1-CCH-2** · P1 — Menu-scrape blocked-target check and write are not atomic, allowing duplicate concurrent Apify runs against the same target
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/MenuApifyScraper.php (fetchStores, fetchHttpStores)
    - **Affects:** Menu scrape jobs, Apify spend budgets, and platform WAF/blocklist reputation — a burst of concurrent jobs for the same store can each pass the blocked-target filter and each issue a billed scrape.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap the read (`Cache::has(CacheKeyGenerator::menuScrapeBlocked(...))`) and the later `Cache::put`/`Cache::forget` write in a single-flight lock per `(platform, normalized URL)`.
        - Pin the lock to `Cache::store('cache_locks')->lock(...)` (or route through `CacheLockService`) with `$lockSeconds` exceeding worst-case Apify/HTTP fetch time and a bounded `$blockSeconds`.
    - **Technical:** Category 1/9 — no single-flight guard spans the check-then-act cycle. `fetchStores()` filters candidate targets with `Cache::has(CacheKeyGenerator::menuScrapeBlocked(...))`, then performs the actual scrape, and only afterward writes the negative-cache marker with `Cache::put($blockedKey, ..., $ttl)`. Two jobs racing on the same target can both pass the `Cache::has` filter before either writes the block, so both claim scrape budget and both bill Apify for the same store. `fetchHttpStores()` has the identical TOCTOU shape.
    - **Plain English:** Two workers can both check "has this store already failed recently?", both see "no", and both go ahead and pay for the same expensive lookup at the same time — like two people checking an empty reservation book at once and both seating a table that's actually already spoken for.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $targets = array_filter(
            $targets,
            fn (array $t) => ! Cache::has(CacheKeyGenerator::menuScrapeBlocked($t['platform'], $t['url'])),
        );
        ```
        ```php
        $blockedKey = CacheKeyGenerator::menuScrapeBlocked($target['platform'], $target['url']);
        if ($mapped !== null) {
            $menus[$key] = $mapped;
            Cache::forget($blockedKey);
        } elseif ($budgetExhausted) {
            Log::info('menu.apify.blocked_cache_skipped_budget', ['platform' => $target['platform'], 'user_id' => $userId]);
        } else {
            Cache::put($blockedKey, ['at' => now()->toIso8601String(), 'reason' => $this->responseRetryable($resp) ? 'blocked' : 'hard_error'], $ttl);
        }
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): WONTFIX — premise debunked, no code change. menuScrapeBlocked is a NEGATIVE cache written ONLY when a scrape FAILS (MenuApifyScraper.php:236, :305) and forgotten on success (:229, :302), so in the success path no marker exists to race on and a single-flight lock around the check/write would prevent ZERO paid runs; the audit read a failure-suppression marker as a claim token. Duplicate paid scrapes are already prevented at the job boundary: MenuFetchJob implements ShouldBeUnique (app/Jobs/Platforms/MenuFetchJob.php:59) with uniqueId() = userId (:91) and uniqueFor = 1800 (:82), and all four dispatchers use Job::dispatch() rather than Bus::dispatch(), so the unique lock is not dropped; plus ApifyBudget::tryClaim(menu) per target before the pool (:183, atomic via DailyCounterClaim, per-actor AND global daily caps) and the RateLimited(platform-connect) middleware (:104). fetchHttpStores() takes no Apify token and claims no budget, so it bills nothing and the money argument cannot apply to it at all. Residual, real but P3 and availability-not-spend: a lost Cache::forget can leave a recovered target negative-cached for blocked_ttl_seconds — a spurious skip bounded by TTL.

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-3** · P2 — Unjittered TTL on malformed-detector-pattern throttle write
    - **Where:** app/Routing/LinkProjector.php (reportMalformedPattern)
    - **Affects:** On-call observability — malformed-detector reports can synchronize expiry and re-alert in bursts fleet-wide; not user-facing data.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Apply `JitteredTtl::applyJitter($ttl)` to the computed TTL before it reaches `Cache::add`.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. `reportMalformedPattern()` computes `$ttl = max(1, (int) config('partna.routing.malformed_pattern_report_ttl_seconds', 3600));` (config key confirmed at `config/partna.php:2340`) and writes it straight through `Cache::add(...)`, bypassing `JitteredTtl`. Every malformed-pattern report logged near the same moment expires in lockstep, producing synchronized re-alert bursts.
    - **Plain English:** Every "don't re-alert on this yet" note is set to expire in exactly one hour. If several are created around the same time, they all expire together and re-fire as a noisy burst instead of trickling in one at a time.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $ttl = max(1, (int) config('partna.routing.malformed_pattern_report_ttl_seconds', 3600));

        if (! Cache::add("routing:malformed-detector:{$detectorId}:{$field}", 1, $ttl)) {
            return;
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-4** · P2 — Staff bulk status update bypasses Eloquent events, so no public-site-cache invalidation runs
    - **Where:** app/Http/Controllers/Api/Staff/UserSiteManagement/StaffUserController.php (bulkUpdateStatus, write app/Http/Controllers/Api/Staff/UserSiteManagement/StaffUserController.php:222-227; read `SiteCacheService::getPublicSitePayload` / `UserCacheService`, invalidated normally by `app/Observers/User/UserObserver.php:66-119`)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepages of every user included in a bulk status change — visitors keep seeing the pre-change active/suspended state until the cache TTL expires.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - After the transaction commits, call `UserCacheService::invalidateUser(...)` (and `SiteCacheInvalidator::touchSite(...)` as needed) for every id in `$existing`, mirroring what `UserObserver::updated()` does for the single-record path.
        - Prefer converting the bulk path to per-model saves (so `UserObserver` fires naturally) if the row count (capped at 100) makes that acceptable; otherwise explicitly replicate the invalidation loop.
    - **Technical:** Category 4 — missing push-invalidation. `bulkUpdateStatus()` performs `User::query()->whereIn('id', $existing)->update(['status' => $status]);` — a query-builder mass update that does **not** fire Eloquent model events. By contrast, the single-record `updateStatus()` and `update()` actions on this same controller go through `$professional->save()`, which *does* trigger `UserObserver::updated()` (confirmed: `app/Observers/User/UserObserver.php:66`, wired with `afterCommit = true`), which calls `UserCacheService::invalidateUser()` and conditionally `SiteCacheInvalidator::touchSite()`. The bulk path is the one write site in this controller that genuinely skips that machinery.
    - **Plain English:** When staff suspend a handful of accounts one at a time, the system quietly refreshes each person's public page. But when staff suspend 80 accounts in one bulk action, that refresh step is skipped for all of them — visitors keep seeing the old "open" page until it happens to expire on its own.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        DB::transaction(function () use ($ids, $status, &$updated, &$missing): void {
            $existing = User::query()->whereIn('id', $ids)->get(['id'])->pluck('id')->all();
            $missing = array_values(array_diff($ids, $existing));

            if (! empty($existing)) {
                User::query()
                    ->whereIn('id', $existing)
                    ->update(['status' => $status]);
                $updated = $existing;
            }
        });
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-5** · P2 — GoogleBusinessEnrichJob inflight marker uses an unjittered literal TTL
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php (handle, `APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL = 900`)
    - **Affects:** `google_business_apify_inflight:{userId}:{placeId}` marker that prevents duplicate paid Apify calls during job retries.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Route the write through `JitteredTtl::applyJitter(self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL)`.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. `Cache::put($inflightKey, now()->toIso8601String(), self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL);` writes a literal 900-second TTL. A burst of jobs writing this marker at the same time expire together, producing a synchronized wave of re-entries into the paid Apify call.
    - **Plain English:** Every "already working on this" note expires after exactly 15 minutes. When a batch of these notes go up together, they all disappear at the same second, and everything tries the expensive scrape again at once instead of trickling back in.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($inflightKey, now()->toIso8601String(), self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL);
        ```

    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php (handle, `APIFY_RESULT_TTL = 1800`)
    - **Affects:** `google_business_apify_result:{userId}:{placeId}` marker that prevents re-billing Apify during the retry window.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Route the write through `JitteredTtl::applyJitter(self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL)`.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. `Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL);` writes a literal 1800-second TTL, synchronizing expiry of paid-call receipts across the fleet and creating a rebilling wave when many jobs complete together.
    - **Plain English:** The "we already paid for this" receipt expires after exactly 30 minutes for everyone. If many receipts are created close together, they all vanish at once and the system re-pays for the same lookups in a burst instead of spreading it out.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-7** · P2 — InstagramMediaUrl failure-marker write uses an unjittered literal TTL
    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/InstagramMediaUrl.php (freshUrl, `FAILED_REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS = 21600`)
    - **Affects:** `instagram-refresh-failed:{shortCode}:{kind}:{position}` negative cache suppressing paid refresh attempts after a failed Instagram URL refresh.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Route the write through `JitteredTtl::applyJitter(self::FAILED_REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS)`.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. `Cache::put($failKey, true, self::FAILED_REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS);` writes a literal 21600-second (6h) TTL. Fleet-wide, negative markers created around the same time all expire simultaneously, producing a synchronized retry wave that burns actor budget.
    - **Plain English:** After a failed photo refresh, the "don't retry yet" note is set for exactly six hours. When lots of these notes are created close together, they all expire at the same moment, and a wave of retries fires at once instead of one at a time.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($failKey, true, self::FAILED_REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-8** · P2 — Menu-scrape negative-cache TTLs are unjittered, synchronizing fleet-wide rescrape bursts
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/MenuApifyScraper.php (fetchStores, fetchHttpStores)
    - **Affects:** Apify/HTTP scrape jobs after a deploy/flush or scheduled expiry; stampede risk on a bounded external budget.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Apply `JitteredTtl::applyJitter($ttl)` at both `Cache::put($blockedKey, ..., $ttl)` sites before writing.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. Both the actor-lane and HTTP-lane writes compute `$ttl = (int) config('partna.menu.blocked_ttl_seconds');` and pass it straight to `Cache::put`, bypassing `JitteredTtl`. Every blocked-target marker expires at the same offset; a deploy or scheduled flush that clears the cache lets every suppressed target become eligible at once, piling requests onto the bounded scrape budget simultaneously.
    - **Plain English:** All the "don't scrape this again yet" notes are set to expire at exactly the same time. When they do — or a restart clears them all together — every store gets re-checked against the paid scraping service at once instead of gradually.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $ttl = (int) config('partna.menu.blocked_ttl_seconds');
        ...
        Cache::put($blockedKey, ['at' => now()->toIso8601String(), 'reason' => $this->responseRetryable($resp) ? 'blocked' : 'hard_error'], $ttl);
        ```
        ```php
        $ttl = (int) config('partna.menu.blocked_ttl_seconds');
        ...
        Cache::put($blockedKey, ['at' => now()->toIso8601String(), 'reason' => 'http_fetch_failed'], $ttl);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-9** · P2 — Previous-website card sweep retires public link cards with no site-cache invalidation
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob.php (handle, write via `ManualMenuWriter::markRemoved()` → `app/Services/Content/ManualPoolWriter.php:100-108`; read `SiteCacheService::getPublicSitePayload`, established invalidation precedent at `app/Jobs/Platforms/MenuFetchJob.php:1454-1469`)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage payload readers — swept link cards remain visible until the cache TTL expires.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - After the sweep loop, when `$swept > 0`, call `app(SiteCacheInvalidator::class)->touchSite(fn () => User::query()->with('site')->find($this->userId)?->site, 'previous-website-card-sweep', ['user_id' => $this->userId]);`, mirroring `MenuFetchJob::bustSiteCache()`.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. `ManualPoolWriter::markRemoved()` mutates `content.items` via a raw query-builder `->update(['removed_at' => ..., 'updated_at' => ...])` (confirmed at `app/Services/Content/ManualPoolWriter.php:100-108`), which bypasses Eloquent model events entirely. `SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob::handle()` calls this writer directly and performs no cache invalidation afterward, even though `MenuFetchJob` establishes the exact same "menu writes bypass observers, so bust explicitly" pattern via `bustSiteCache()` for structurally identical content-pool mutations.
    - **Plain English:** This job quietly removes old website cards that duplicate the owner's own site, but never tells the public page's saved snapshot to refresh — like taking flyers off a wall without telling anyone the wall changed, so visitors keep seeing the old photo of the wall until it happens to be refreshed for an unrelated reason.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        foreach ($rows as $row) {
            if ($this->hostOf((string) $row->url) === $host) {
                $writer->markRemoved((string) $row->id);
                $swept++;
            }
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-10** · P2 — MediaMirror updates cached public media URLs/storage paths without site-cache invalidation
    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/MediaMirror.php (mirror(), lines ~99-101 and ~326-330; read `SiteCacheService::getPublicSitePayload`)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage payload readers — refreshed Instagram CDN URLs or newly-stored R2 media paths can remain stale in the cached site payload.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - After a successful `content.media_assets` update (both the refreshed `source_url` branch and the final `storage_path` landing update), call the matching site cache invalidation for the owning user/site.
        - Confirmed: `MediaMirror.php` imports no `SiteCacheInvalidator`/cache service at all — this needs to be added as a constructor dependency.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. `MediaMirror::mirror()` writes directly to `content.media_assets` via `DB::connection('pgsql')->table(...)->update(...)` in at least two places — once refreshing an expired `source_url`, once landing the final `storage_path`/mime/size. Both are query-builder writes that bypass Eloquent model events, so no observer-driven cache bust fires. The file has zero references to any cache-invalidation service.
    - **Plain English:** When the system rescues an expired Instagram photo link or finishes saving a fresh copy of an image, it updates the database but never tells the public page's saved snapshot to refresh — visitors can keep seeing a broken or outdated image link until the snapshot happens to refresh for some other reason.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        DB::connection('pgsql')->table('content.media_assets')
            ->where('id', $assetId)
            ->update(['source_url' => $fresh]);
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-CCH-11** · P3 — Ad-hoc cache key concatenation for the malformed-pattern throttle
    - **Where:** app/Routing/LinkProjector.php (reportMalformedPattern)
    - **Affects:** Maintainability/key-generation consistency — no user-data exposure or known incorrect read today; drift risk if another path ever needs to read/forget this key.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `CacheKeyGenerator::routingMalformedDetector($detectorId, $field)` and use it here instead of inline concatenation.
    - **Technical:** Category 8. The key is built with inline string interpolation rather than through `CacheKeyGenerator`. Only one write site exists today, so there's no active drift, but any future reader/forgetter reconstructing the string independently risks a silent mismatch.
    - **Plain English:** The label on this cache entry is hand-typed instead of coming from the shared label-maker. It works today because only one place writes it, but if someone later needs to read or clear it and types the label slightly differently, the two copies silently stop matching.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::add("routing:malformed-detector:{$detectorId}:{$field}", 1, $ttl)
        ```

    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/InstagramMediaUrl.php (freshUrl)

        - Add a `CacheKeyGenerator` method for this failed-refresh key and use it at the one existing call site.

    - **Plain English:** This "don't retry yet" label is also hand-written in the code rather than pulled from the shared label book — fine while one place uses it, risky the moment a second place needs to read or clear the same note.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $failKey = "instagram-refresh-failed:{$shortCode}:{$kind}:{$position}";
        ```

## Suggested Bundled Sessions

- **Bundle 1 — Unjittered literal TTLs across platform/routing negative caches:** #CCH-3, #CCH-5, #CCH-6, #CCH-7, #CCH-8
    - **Why grouped:** identical root cause (literal int TTL passed straight to `Cache::put`/`Cache::add`, bypassing `JitteredTtl::applyJitter`) across five otherwise-unrelated files — one mechanical fix pattern applied five times.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 2 — Missing push-invalidation on content/media write paths:** #CCH-9, #CCH-10
    - **Why grouped:** both are query-builder writes to `content.*` tables that bypass Eloquent observers and need an explicit `SiteCacheInvalidator::touchSite(...)` call, following the exact precedent already established in `MenuFetchJob::bustSiteCache()`.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 3 — Ad-hoc cache key concatenation cleanup:** #CCH-11, #CCH-12
    - **Why grouped:** same low-risk pattern (inline string key instead of `CacheKeyGenerator` helper); mechanical, no behavior change.
    - **Model:** Plan: Sonnet · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

## Standalone — do NOT bundle

- **#CCH-1 — Workplace identity-mirror cache-then-DB race** · standalone: P1, touches cross-model cache-invalidation ordering on a live observer chain (`WorkplaceObserver` + `UserObserver`) — needs its own plan and review to avoid re-introducing double-invalidation or missing the `afterCommit` semantics.
- **#CCH-2 — Menu-scrape TOCTOU race allowing duplicate paid Apify runs** · standalone: P1, requires introducing a new lock scope (`cache_locks` connection) around an external paid-API call path — genuinely gnarly concurrency change, not a mechanical fix.
- **#CCH-4 — Staff bulk status update missing push-invalidation** · standalone: touches an auth/staff-privileged bulk-mutation endpoint affecting up to 100 user accounts at once; needs its own review to confirm the invalidation loop doesn't reintroduce an N+1 or a timing issue under `require.aal2`.

**Branch:** development

- P0 Blockers: 0 of 0 complete

- P2 Medium: 0 of 0 complete

---

No findings survive adjudication for this chunk's scope.

DeepSeek's draft returned no findings for `routes/api/platforms.php` and `routes/console.php`. Independent review confirms this is correct — neither file is a candidate surface for this lens:

- `routes/api/platforms.php` registers per-user dashboard integration endpoints (Fresha, Square, Instagram, Apple, Eventbrite, Humanitix, generic registry-driven platforms, menu content management). Every route in the file sits behind the `user.api` + `EnforcePendingDeletionReadOnly` + `throttle:authenticated` stack — these are authenticated first-party client calls, not inbound webhooks/hooks from an external sender, and carry no HMAC verification, idempotency-anchor, or bot-token concerns in scope for this lens.
- `routes/console.php` is the Artisan scheduler definition (`Schedule::command(...)` / `Schedule::job(...)` / `Schedule::call(...)`) — internally triggered cron, not an inbound callback path. It receives no external POST body and has no signature/idempotency surface to audit.

The actual callback surface this lens targets — `SupabaseAuthHookController`, `SupabaseEmailHookController`, `VerifySupabaseHookSignature`, `StandardWebhookVerifier`, `IdempotencyKey` middleware, `VerifyBotToken`, and the hook-route registrations in `routes/api.php` (Groups A–F in the lens spec) — was not part of this chunk's scope and was not scanned here. A clean result for this chunk should not be read as clearance for those files; they require their own scan pass.

# Transaction Boundary Correctness Audit — 2026-08-27

**Branch:** development
**Lens:** Transaction boundary correctness — `DB::transaction`/`DB::beginTransaction` discipline: no external I/O, queue dispatches, cache writes, or event side-effects inside a transaction; bounded scope; safe retry/nesting; consistent lock ordering.
**Pipeline:** scan-tier draft by `deepseek-v4-pro`, adjudicated by `claude-opus-4.6`
**Source files audited:**
- app/Routing/SourceReconciler.php
- app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php
- app/Services/Design/DesignKitAutopilot.php
- app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php
- app/Observers/Core/WorkplaceObserver.php
- (full scope group list per lens spec — Groups A–G)

## Progress

- P0 Blockers: 0 of 0 complete
- P1 High: 0 of 0 complete
- P2 Medium: 0 of 1 complete
- P3 Low: 0 of 1 complete

---

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-TXN-1** · P2 — Per-site popularity upsert and fade-out delete are not wrapped in one transaction
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:228-248 (`handle()` per-site write block)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage ranking/order readers of `analytics.content_popularity_scores` — a failure between the upsert and the deletes serves a mix of newly-computed scores and stale rows that should have been faded out, until the next 15-minute sweep self-heals it.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap the per-site `upsert($rows, ...)` and the subsequent `foreach ($deletes ...)` delete loop in a single `DB::transaction(fn() => ...)`.
        - Keep the transaction limited to those two write statements — no external I/O, cache writes, queue dispatches, or notifications inside (there are none here today; keep it that way if this block is ever extended).
    - **Technical:** The command treats the score upsert and the fade-out deletion of stale keys as one logical "replace this site's score set" operation, but executes them as two independent autocommit statements (`upsert(...)` then a per-content-type `delete()` loop). If the PHP process dies, the command times out, or the delete throws between the two, the new rows are already committed while rows that should have been removed remain visible to readers — `PoolResolver`/ranking consumers would observe a mix of fresh and stale content-popularity rows. The scheduled sweep (every 15 minutes, per the class docblock) will eventually reconcile it on the next run, so this is a transient-staleness bug rather than permanent corruption, but wrapping the pair in a transaction removes the window entirely at negligible cost since both statements already target the same connection and no I/O sits between them. This is a happy-path-tested command (`--dry-run` and normal runs are exercised); no test simulates a mid-write failure, so this gap is invisible in green CI by design.
    - **Plain English:** Think of this like updating a shop's price list and then, in a separate step, clearing out the old discontinued items. If the till crashes between the two steps, customers briefly see both the new prices and some items that should already be gone. Wrapping both changes in one envelope means they either both happen together or neither happens — no in-between state for a visitor to see.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($rows !== []) {
            DB::connection('pgsql')
                ->table('analytics.content_popularity_scores')
                ->upsert(
                    $rows,
                    ['site_id', 'content_type', 'content_key'],
                    ['score', 'rank', 'computed_at'],
                );
            $rowsWritten += count($rows);
        }

        // Faded-out keys — remove instead of leaving frozen stale rows.
        foreach ($deletes as $contentType => $keys) {
            DB::connection('pgsql')
                ->table('analytics.content_popularity_scores')
                ->where('site_id', $site->id)
                ->where('content_type', $contentType)
                ->whereIn('content_key', $keys)
                ->delete();
            $rowsDeleted += count($keys);
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-TXN-2** · P3 — `recordCapBlock()` calls a lock-dependent helper outside the transaction its own docblock requires
    - **Where:** app/Routing/SourceReconciler.php:301-324 (`recordCapBlock()`), calling `upsertIntent()` → `advanceLiveIntent()` (documented contract at line 415)
    - **Affects:** The legacy Google Business / Instagram cap-block harvest lane (`LinkRouter.php:453,529` are the only callers) — a race is possible only if two of these calls (or one of these racing `reconcile()`'s own transactional write) land for the exact same `(user, surface, identifier)` triple at the same instant.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap the `$this->upsertIntent(...)` call inside `recordCapBlock()` in a `DB::transaction(...)`, matching the shape `reconcile()` already uses around the same helper (line 165).
        - Add a regression test exercising two concurrent `recordCapBlock()` calls for the same triple to confirm the "impossible case" `RuntimeException` in `upsertIntent()` can no longer fire.
    - **Technical:** `advanceLiveIntent()`'s own docblock states plainly: "Race-free ONLY inside a transaction that holds that lock … never call this outside one." `upsertIntent()` calls it twice (initial advance, and the lost-race re-read), relying on the row lock from its own `UPDATE` being held until commit so the re-read is safe. `reconcile()` honours this by wrapping the whole sequence in `DB::transaction(...)`. `recordCapBlock()` calls `upsertIntent()` directly with no surrounding transaction, so in Postgres autocommit mode each statement's lock is released at statement end — the re-read is no longer guaranteed race-free, and a genuinely concurrent write for the same triple could hit the `throw new \RuntimeException("Could not upsert source intent for …")` invariant-failure branch instead of the intended idempotent outcome. This is real-but-narrow: it requires two writers to race on the identical `(user, surface_key, identifier)` key, which only the two named `LinkRouter` call sites reach, and the failure mode is a thrown exception (a job/request failure surfaced to Nightwatch), not silent data corruption — consistent with a lock-ordering/concurrency-hygiene gap rather than a routinely-hit correctness bug.
    - **Plain English:** This method reads a shared ledger, writes a row, and reads it back — but the code comment right next to it warns "this only works safely if you're holding a lock the whole time," and this caller doesn't hold that lock. In the rare case where two processes try to record the same blocked suggestion at the exact same moment, one of them could hit an unexpected error instead of quietly agreeing with the other.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public function recordCapBlock(
            User $user,
            string $surfaceKey,
            string $routingClass,
            string $identifier,
            string $url,
            string $incumbentConnectionId,
            string $origin = 'google_business',
        ): void {
            $iri = $this->canonicaliser->canonicalize($url);
            $placement = new Placement(Verdict::Hold, $surfaceKey, $identifier, 'cap_reached');

            $this->upsertIntent(
                $user,
                $placement,
                RoutingContext::forUser($user, $origin),
                $iri,
                $routingClass,
                $identifier,
                Verdict::Hold,
                'cap_reached',
                $incumbentConnectionId,
            );
        }
        ```
        ```php
        // Race-free ONLY inside a transaction that holds that lock
        // (LIFE-16's DB::transaction in reconcile()); never call this outside one.
        private function advanceLiveIntent(User $user, ?string $surfaceKey, string $identifier, array $fields): ?string
        ```

    - **Why grouped:** single-file, single-method fix.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.
- **Bundle 2 — SourceReconciler cap-block transaction wrap:** #TXN-2
    - **Why grouped:** single-file, single-method fix.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

## Progress

---

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W1-DINT-1** · P1 — Recycled email leaks a previous account's early-access PII through the DSAR export
    - **Resolved:** Fixed 2026-08-28 as a duplicate of #W1-PRIV-1 — its first two bullets are essentially the implemented design, and the `user_id` FK it asks for has existed since the 2026-07-26 baseline. Its third bullet (backfill/quarantine rows whose `email_lc` no longer matches the current owner of that email) was REJECTED as unimplementable: there is no oracle for who currently holds an email address. The withheld-marker bucket is the answer to what it was reaching for.
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:1206-1229 (`streamEarlyAccessSignups`), `COVERED_PII_TABLES` docblock
    - **Affects:** Current holder of a recycled email requesting a GDPR export; the previous owner's early-access signup details; Article 15 compliance posture.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Bind `core.early_access_signups` to the durable account via a `user_id` FK where a claim has occurred, rather than joining purely on `email_lc`.
        - Scope the DSAR query by `user_id` first, falling back to `email_lc` only for signups that never progressed to a claimed account.
        - Add a migration to backfill/quarantine rows whose `email_lc` no longer matches the current owner of that email.
    - **Technical:** `streamEarlyAccessSignups()` matches purely on `->where('email_lc', $emailLc)` with no ownership check. The method's own docblock documents that `EarlyAccessService::signupFromMarketing()` upserts by `email_lc` via `firstOrCreate`, and once a row has progressed past `waitlist` a resubmission under a recycled email is a silent no-op — the row keeps the FORMER occupant's `type`, `status`, `invited_at`, and `signed_up_at`. Because the DSAR export has no ownership FK to check against, a current account holder whose email was previously used by an unrelated signup receives that former occupant's early-access data in their own export. This is a live, self-documented gap ("no schema-level fix yet") rather than a hypothetical.
    - **Plain English:** Imagine a phone number gets reassigned to a new owner, but the old owner's voicemail greeting is still attached. When the new owner asks "what information do you have tied to my number," the system hands back the previous person's greeting because it only looked up the number, not who currently holds it.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        /**
         * Early-access signup record. No FK to core.users — joined only by email_lc —
         * so the row persists after signup. ...
         * Email-recycle note: EarlyAccessService::signupFromMarketing() upserts by
         * email_lc via firstOrCreate, ... Once a row has
         * progressed to invited or signed_up, a resubmission under a recycled email
         * is a silent no-op — the row keeps the FORMER occupant's type, status,
         * invited_at, and signed_up_at, not just created_at/id. Larger leak than the
         * streamEmailSubscriptions case; no schema-level fix yet.
         */
        ->table('core.early_access_signups')
        ->where('email_lc', $emailLc)
        ```

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-2** · P2 — `content.offers.platform` has a documented three-value vocabulary but no DB CHECK constraint
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260826200000_offers_per_item_platform_links.sql:8-13, 36-39
    - **Affects:** `content.offers` rows and any platform-specific ordering/linkout logic that branches on `platform`.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `ALTER TABLE "content"."offers" ADD CONSTRAINT "offers_platform_check" CHECK ("platform" IN ('square', 'uber-eats', 'doordash'));` in a follow-up migration.
        - If `ConstraintVocabularyLockstepTest` (used elsewhere in this same commit series for the `event_item` family) covers this vocabulary, extend it to `offers.platform`.
    - **Technical:** The migration documents `platform` as a menu-registry slug with exactly three legal values, but adds the column as plain `text NULL` with no `CHECK`. Postgres will accept any string from direct SQL, a future connector, or a typo'd projector change. This is a category-4 schema/app drift gap — the DB should enforce the vocabulary it documents, not rely solely on `MenuProjectionMapper` to write clean values.
    - **Plain English:** The database has a field for "which ordering platform," but nothing stops a typo or a bad import from writing something other than the three real platforms. The database should reject anything that isn't one of the three, the same way a form with a dropdown won't accept free text.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        --   platform     — menu-registry slug ('square' | 'uber-eats' |
        --                  'doordash') labelling which ordering platform the
        --                  offer belongs to.
        ALTER TABLE "content"."offers"
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "platform" text NULL,
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "item_url" text NULL,
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "external_ref" text NULL;
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-3** · P2 — Email-subscription upsert overwrites the prior subscriber's data when an email is recycled
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:1167-1171 (`streamEmailSubscriptions` docblock)
    - **Affects:** Former/subsequent users sharing a recycled email; `notifications.email_subscriptions` accuracy and retention.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add an owner FK from `notifications.email_subscriptions.user_id` to `core.users.id` in a follow-up migration and treat `email_lc` as an index, not sole identity.
        - Prefer `user_id` ownership in the writer's upsert key; keep `(list_key, email_lc)` only for the anonymous/global-row case.
    - **Technical:** The writer upserts by `(list_key, email_lc)`, so when an email is recycled the row CONTENT is overwritten with the new user's data — only `id`/`created_at` survive from the prior occupant. This is a documented, self-acknowledged gap ("Bounded leak, mostly metadata. Schema-level fix is to add an owner FK; that's a follow-up.") — a data-accuracy/loss risk for the former subscriber, distinct from #DINT-1 in that the leak direction is loss-of-prior-data rather than disclosure-of-prior-data.
    - **Plain English:** Reusing the same room key for a new hotel guest overwrites the previous guest's stay record. When that earlier guest later asks for their records, only a timestamp is left — their actual data is gone, replaced by the new guest's.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
         * Note on email-recycle: writers upsert by (list_key, email_lc), so when
         * an email is recycled (rare today) the row CONTENT is overwritten with
         * the new user's data; only created_at and id are preserved from the
         * prior occupant. Bounded leak, mostly metadata. Schema-level fix is to
         * add an owner FK; that's a follow-up.
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-4** · P2 — Workplace identity mirror dual-write can silently diverge with no rollback or retry
    - **Where:** app/Observers/Core/WorkplaceObserver.php:98-140 (`mirrorIdentityFields`)
    - **Affects:** Business Partna accounts with `workplace_brand_is_site_identity`; consistency of public contact/bio/address fields between `site.workplaces` and `core.users`.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap the workplace save and the `$user->save()` mirror write in one DB transaction, or move the mirror to a small retryable queued job.
        - Keep the `report()`/log for visibility, but surface a reconciliation path (e.g. a scheduled command that diffs mirrored columns) rather than only logging and moving on.
    - **Technical:** `WorkplaceObserver::$afterCommit = true`, so `saved()` fires after the workplace row is already committed; `mirrorIdentityFields()` then performs a second, independent `$user->save()`. The `catch (\Throwable $e)` swallows every exception, calls `report()` and `Log::warning`, and returns normally — there is no rollback of the workplace write (already committed) and no retry of the mirror. A transient failure (deadlock, connection blip) leaves the workplace and user rows permanently divergent on 8 identity fields with no automatic reconciliation.
    - **Plain English:** The system writes an address change to one record, then tries to copy it to a second record. If the copy fails, it just makes a note to itself and moves on — so the two records can end up showing different contact details for the same business indefinitely, until someone edits the field again.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public bool $afterCommit = true;

        private function mirrorIdentityFields(Workplace $workplace): void
        {
            try {
                $user = $workplace->site?->user;
                // ...
                if ($dirty) {
                    $user->save();
                }
            } catch (\Throwable $e) {
                report($e);
                Log::warning('WorkplaceObserver identity-mirror failed', [
                    'site_id' => $workplace->site_id,
                    'message' => $e->getMessage(),
                ]);
            }
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-5** · P2 — `content.item_variants.image_url` is persisted without `SecretParams::minimiseUrl()`
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:2334 (`replaceCollections` → `$variantRows`)
    - **Affects:** `content.item_variants` rows; product variant images; anyone whose variant image URLs carry signed CDN tokens or tracking query strings.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap `image_url` in `SecretParams::minimiseUrl()` in the `$variantRows` builder.
        - Add `'f_review'`-style coverage: check whether any other URL-bearing collection column added since the `URL_COLUMNS` denylist was introduced needs the same treatment.
    - **Technical:** In the same method, offer URLs ARE minimised (`'url' => SecretParams::minimiseUrl($offer['url'] ?? null)`, `'item_url' => SecretParams::minimiseUrl(...)`), but `image_url` — added later (migration 20260813100003, per the inline comment) — is written as `$entry['image_url'] ?? null` with no scrubbing. This bypasses the PRIV-5 URL-minimisation mechanism for a URL-bearing column and can persist signed/tracking parameters verbatim.
    - **Plain English:** The same cleanup process that strips tracking codes from product links wasn't applied to product-image links added later. Those image links go into the database exactly as received, tokens and all.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        'image_url' => $entry['image_url'] ?? null,
        ```
        ```php
        'url' => SecretParams::minimiseUrl($offer['url'] ?? null),
        'item_url' => SecretParams::minimiseUrl($offer['item_url'] ?? null),
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-6** · P2 — `f_review.author_uri` is a URL-bearing column but is missing from the minimisation denylist
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:64, 77-84 (`SINGLETON_FACETS`, `URL_COLUMNS`)
    - **Affects:** `content.f_review` rows; review author URIs; reviewers whose Google contributor-profile links carry query parameters.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `'author_uri'` to `URL_COLUMNS['f_review']` alongside the existing `'author_photo_url'`.
        - Backfill/minimise any already-persisted `content.f_review.author_uri` values.
    - **Technical:** `singletonFacetRow()` only applies `SecretParams::minimiseUrl()` to a column when the `facet => column` pair is listed in `URL_COLUMNS`. `f_review.author_uri` was added to `SINGLETON_FACETS` (slice 6, migration 20260813110000) as a writable column, but was never added to `URL_COLUMNS`, even though `author_photo_url` — the same kind of permanent reviewer-identifying link — is. `DataExportPayloadBuilder::streamContentFReview()` independently treats `author_uri` as sensitive enough to withhold from export entirely ("it is a permanent link to the reviewer's Google contributor profile, so it identifies them at least as directly as author_name"), confirming the column is genuinely PII-bearing and the omission from `URL_COLUMNS` is an oversight, not a deliberate choice.
    - **Plain English:** The system has a tool that strips tracking codes and tokens out of links before saving them. A new "reviewer profile link" field was added to the list of things the system is allowed to save, but nobody added it to the list of things that must be scrubbed first — so it's saved raw.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        'f_review' => ['author_name', 'author_photo_url', 'author_uri', 'rating', 'text', 'reviewed_at'],
        ```
        ```php
        'f_review' => ['author_photo_url'],
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-7** · P2 — `markRemoved()` leaves an item soft-deleted but its slug still allocated if slug-release fails
    - **Where:** app/Services/Content/ManualPoolWriter.php:100-108 (`markRemoved`)
    - **Affects:** Content items in `ContentItemSlugAllocator::SLUGGED_KINDS`; user-facing permalinks; the deletion API surface.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap `$this->freeSlug($itemId)` in `try { ... } catch (\Throwable $e) { report($e); }`, mirroring the try/catch already inside `remintSlug()` (used by the sibling `clearRemoved()`).
    - **Technical:** `markRemoved()` sets `content.items.removed_at` and immediately calls `freeSlug()` with no exception handling. If `freeSlug()` throws, the item is already marked removed at the DB level, but `idx_item_slugs_unique (user_id, slug)` is non-partial (per the method's own docblock: "even a retired row squats its name"), so the slug stays permanently reserved and the caller receives a 500 despite the deletion having succeeded. `clearRemoved()`'s mirror-image call (`remintSlug()`) already catches its own failures for exactly this "must not fail a user-requested state change" reason; `markRemoved()` does not.
    - **Plain English:** Deleting a file while its name stays reserved blocks anyone from ever reusing that name. If the "release the name" step hits a glitch, the person sees an error even though the delete itself worked. The failure should be logged quietly, not surfaced as a broken deletion.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        DB::connection('pgsql')->table('content.items')
            ->where('id', $itemId)
            ->whereNull('removed_at')
            ->update(['removed_at' => $removedAt ?? now(), 'updated_at' => now()]);

        $this->freeSlug($itemId);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-8** · P2 — Identity advisory lock releases before `writeFacets()`/`refreshItemCaches()`, allowing writes against a since-merged item
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:807-827 (`withIdentityLock()` docblock)
    - **Affects:** `content.items`, `content.f_*`, and collection facets when two identity-writing operations for the same user/kind race.
    - **Effort:** L (~1–2d)
    - **What to do:**
        - Extend the identity-locked transaction to cover `writeFacets()`/`refreshItemCaches()`, or re-validate the target item id still exists/is unmerged immediately before those writes.
        - Add a Postgres-lane race test (`tests/Postgres/`) that forces a merge between `resolveItems()` committing and `writeFacets()` running, asserting either a clean no-op or a surfaced error rather than an orphaned write.
    - **Technical:** The method's own docblock states the advisory lock only serialises writers that take it, and explicitly lists `writeFacets()`/`refreshItemCaches()` as running AFTER the lock commits, "against item ids a later resolve may already have merged away (pre-existing, unchanged here)." This is a genuinely still-open race for the same user's own concurrent identity-writing calls (e.g. a double-submit), independent of the three hard-delete call sites the same docblock separately documents as currently unreachable/inert. A losing writer's facet insert can hit a `23503` FK violation against a merged-away item id.
    - **Plain English:** The system locks a record while deciding who owns what, then unlocks before actually saving the details. If two save operations for the same person overlap, one can finish deciding, get unlocked, and then try to save details onto a record the other operation has already merged away — causing an error instead of a clean save.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        * WHAT THIS DOES NOT COVER. An advisory lock only serialises writers that take it, and four
        * identity mutators still do not:
        *   - writeFacets() and refreshItemCaches() run AFTER this commits, against item ids a later
        *     resolve may already have merged away (pre-existing, unchanged here);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-9** · P2 — `storedOrigin()` reads an `item_tag` without scoping to the specific source item, can return the wrong `link_origin`
    - **Where:** app/Services/Content/LinkPoolWriter.php:208-220 (`storedOrigin`)
    - **Affects:** Content link items and their `link_origin` tags; the previous-website sweep that relies on these tags to distinguish manual vs. scraped cards from deletion.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Scope the `content.item_tags` join to the specific source-item row rather than just the item: `->join('content.item_tags as t', function ($j) { $j->on('t.item_id', '=', 'si.item_id')->on('t.source_item_id', '=', 'si.id'); })`. **Not** `t.source_id = si.source_id` — `cs.kind = 'manual'` already pins the query to the single manual `content.sources` row per user (`idx_content_sources_manual`), so `source_id` is constant across every manual coord for that user and would not disambiguate two coords merged onto the same item.
        - Add a regression test with two manual coords resolved onto one item, each carrying a different `link_origin` tag, asserting `storedOrigin()` returns the tag for the correct coord.
    - **Technical:** `storedOrigin()` joins `content.item_tags as t` on `t.item_id = si.item_id` only. `content.item_tags.source_item_id` was added in the 2026-08-26 migration (`20260826120000_facet_source_item_origin.sql`) precisely to resolve this class of ambiguity — its own comment states "two hand-added items bound to one item are indistinguishable" without it — and `ProjectionWriter::replaceCollections()` (line ~2306) already populates `source_item_id` on every `item_tags` write via `$originByCoord[$entry['coord']]`. `storedOrigin()` was not updated to use the new column, so a `link_origin` tag from a different source_item on the same item can be returned and then re-supplied on the next write (`writeFacets()` REPLACES an item's tag set per write). If a manual card is mis-tagged `scrape`, `SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob` could retire a card the owner typed by hand.
    - **Plain English:** Two different filed documents about the same case can each carry a different sticky note, but the code asks for "the sticky note on this case" without saying which document's note it wants — so it can grab the wrong one. A recent database change added the exact tool needed to ask for the right one; this file just hasn't started using it yet.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $origin = DB::connection('pgsql')->table('content.source_items as si')
            ->join('content.sources as cs', 'cs.id', '=', 'si.source_id')
            ->join('content.item_tags as t', 't.item_id', '=', 'si.item_id')
            ->where('cs.user_id', $userId)
            ->where('cs.kind', 'manual')
            ->where('si.coord', $coord)
            ->where('t.tag_type', 'link_origin')
            ->value('t.tag');
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-10** · P3 — Four new `source_item_id` FKs lack a single-column index for cascade-delete lookups
    - **Where:** supabase/migrations/20260826120000_facet_source_item_origin.sql:34-48; 20260826120001-4_facet_origin_idx_*.sql
    - **Affects:** `content.item_media`, `content.offers`, `content.item_tags`, `content.item_variants` if a parent `content.source_items` row is ever hard-deleted.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add a dedicated `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS "idx_item_media_source_item_id" ON "content"."item_media" ("source_item_id");` (and the equivalent for the other three tables), each in its own migration file per this repo's one-`CONCURRENTLY`-per-file convention.
    - **Technical:** The four new columns are `REFERENCES "content"."source_items" ("id") ON DELETE CASCADE`, but the only indexes added (`idx_item_media_origin` etc.) are composite `(item_id, source_item_id)`, built to serve `replaceCollections()`'s own scoped-delete query. Because `item_id` leads, Postgres cannot efficiently use these composite indexes to answer a `source_item_id = <id>` predicate alone — which is exactly the lookup `ON DELETE CASCADE` performs when a `content.source_items` row is removed. Today this is low-urgency: `content.source_items` rows are marked via `removed_at` far more often than hard-deleted, and the tables are small pre-launch. Worth adding before either table grows or a hard-delete path becomes common.
    - **Plain English:** The database can quickly find related records by "which item," but not by "which specific source record" on its own. If a source record is ever permanently deleted, cleanup has to scan the whole table looking for matches instead of using a shortcut — slow today only because the tables are still small.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        ALTER TABLE "content"."item_media"
            ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "source_item_id" uuid NULL
            REFERENCES "content"."source_items" ("id") ON DELETE CASCADE;
        ```
        ```sql
        CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS "idx_item_media_origin" ON "content"."item_media" ("item_id", "source_item_id");
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-DINT-11** · P3 — `ensureContentSource()` uses a non-atomic check-then-insert, though the DB already enforces the invariant it's trying to protect
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:379-399 (`ensureContentSource`); supabase/migrations/20260727140000_content_schema.sql:40-41
    - **Affects:** `content.sources` rows for connector-backed sources; concurrent projection runs for the same `connection_id`.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Rewrite `ensureContentSource()` to use `insertOrIgnore()` + a re-read, mirroring `ensureManualSource()`'s pattern immediately below it in the same file.
        - Add a regression test simulating two concurrent `ensureContentSource()` calls for the same `connection_id`, asserting both return the same id with no exception.
    - **Technical:** DeepSeek's original framing of this as a missing-unique-index gap is incorrect — `idx_content_sources_connection` (a `UNIQUE INDEX ... ("connection_id") WHERE ("connection_id" IS NOT NULL)`) already exists in `20260727140000_content_schema.sql:40-41`, so a genuine duplicate row can never be persisted; this is not a data-corruption risk. The real, narrower gap: `ensureContentSource()` does `SELECT ... value('id')` then `INSERT`, with no `insertOrIgnore()`. Under a genuine race, the losing writer's plain `INSERT` throws an uncaught unique-violation exception instead of gracefully resolving to the winner's row the way `ensureManualSource()` does. This degrades a race from "silently correct" to "one caller gets an unhandled 500," which is a reliability gap, not a data-integrity one — hence P3, not the P2 DeepSeek assigned.
    - **Plain English:** Two employees can't actually end up with two ID cards for the same customer — the filing cabinet itself refuses a duplicate. But if they both try to file at the exact same moment, one of them gets an error message thrown in their face instead of being told "someone already filed that, here's the card." Not a data problem, just a rough edge.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $existing = DB::table('content.sources')->where('connection_id', $connectionId)->value('id');
        if ($existing !== null) {
            return (string) $existing;
        }

        $id = (string) Str::uuid();
        DB::table('content.sources')->insert([
            'id' => $id,
            'user_id' => $userId,
            'kind' => 'connection',
            'connection_id' => $connectionId,
            'label' => $label,
            'priority' => 100,
            'created_at' => now(),
            'updated_at' => now(),
        ]);

        return $id;
        ```
        ```sql
        CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "idx_content_sources_connection" ON "content"."sources" ("connection_id")
            WHERE ("connection_id" IS NOT NULL);
        ```
