# Job/Queue Correctness Audit — 2026-08-27

**Branch:** development
**Lens:** Job/Queue Correctness: idempotency, retry safety, ShouldBeUnique, missing `$this->fail()`, retry storms
**Pipeline:** scan-tier draft by `deepseek-v4-pro`, adjudicated by `claude-opus-4.6`
**Source files audited:**
- app/Jobs/Platforms/CommerceProbeJob.php
- app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php
- app/Jobs/Platforms/MenuFetchJob.php
- app/Jobs/Platforms/SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob.php
- app/Services/Platforms/GoogleBusinessApifyScraper.php (adjacent, verified)
- app/Observers/Core/WorkplaceObserver.php (adjacent, verified)
- config/partna.php, config/horizon.php (verified)
- app/Console/Commands/BackfillStorefrontFaviconsCommand.php
- app/Console/Commands/CheckStuckSourceIntentsCommand.php
- app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php
- app/Console/Commands/RegistryDumpCommand.php
- app/Console/Commands/SettleConnectedSourceIntentsCommand.php
- app/Mail/Branding/EmailBrandDefaults.php

## Progress

- P0 Blockers: 0 of 0 complete
- P1 High: 0 of 0 complete
- P2 Medium: 0 of 3 complete
- P3 Low: 0 of 1 complete

---

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-JOB-1** · P2 — CommerceProbeJob's top-level resolution catch discards a possibly-transient failure with no retry path
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/CommerceProbeJob.php:139-146
    - **Affects:** Users whose link resolution hits a systemic error (DB outage, seeder service exception) during scan/paste/accept flows — they silently get a plain custom-link card instead of the richer resolution (store connect, product seed, event seed) the link actually deserved.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - In the outer `catch (Throwable $e)`, keep `report($e)` (this already reaches Nightwatch via the exception-reporting pipeline) but distinguish infrastructure-shaped exceptions (DB/connection errors) from expected per-URL resolution misses.
        - For infra-shaped exceptions, `$this->fail($e)` instead of falling through to `seedCustom()` — a DB outage should be retried by Horizon, not permanently downgraded to a fallback card on the first attempt.
        - Leave the fallback-to-`seedCustom()` behavior for genuine per-URL resolution misses (e.g. a 404 or an unparseable page), which is working as designed ("every miss falls through — nothing vanishes").
    - **Technical:** Category 2. The `catch (Throwable $e)` around the resolution `match` swallows every exception type uniformly — a transient DB outage inside `EventsSeeder`/`StoreBrandSeeder`/`GenericShopScraper` looks identical to "the page didn't parse." `report($e)` is called, which does reach Nightwatch (Laravel's `report()` helper flows through the same exception-handling pipeline Nightwatch hooks for alerting), so this is not fully invisible — but the job still completes "successfully" from Horizon's perspective, meaning `$tries=2`/`$backoff=[30]` never get a chance to retry a genuinely transient failure, and the link is permanently downgraded to a plain custom-link card on the very first attempt.
    - **Plain English:** When something goes wrong while figuring out what a link points to, this job quietly gives up and files it as a plain link instead of trying again. If the problem was temporary — like a brief hiccup talking to the database — the system never gets a second chance to do it properly; the wrong (simpler) outcome just sticks.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            report($e);
            $resolved = false;
        }

        if (! $resolved && ! $this->suggestOnly) {
            $links->seedCustom($user, $this->url);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-JOB-2** · P2 — GoogleBusinessEnrichJob's vendor-enrichment failure is logged only, never reported — invisible to Nightwatch during a systemic outage
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php:200-224
    - **Affects:** Google Business enrichment completeness across all users if the Apify actor or website-harvest path degrades systemically (e.g. actor rented-out revoked, budget exhausted); engineers get no proactive signal.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add a `report(new \RuntimeException(...))` call alongside the existing `Log::warning` in the `$enrichment === null && $harvest === []` branch — this preserves the deliberate "soft failure, don't hard-fail the job" design (the dashboard already surfaces `apify_status = 'unavailable'` to the user, and a reconnect retries), while making a systemic vendor outage visible to Nightwatch the same way `GoogleBusinessApifyScraper::fetch()` already does for 5xx responses (`report(new \RuntimeException(...))` at line 74 of that file).
        - Do not convert this to `$this->fail()` — the job's own design (documented in the surrounding comments) is correct that a single place's soft-failure should not consume a retry attempt or block a user-initiated reconnect.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. This is an intentional soft-failure path (the code comment states "No hard fail — the core card is unaffected and a re-connect can retry"), and the job is right not to call `$this->fail()` here. But per this repo's own observability doctrine ("a failure that needs attention must throw or `$this->fail($e)`; bare `Log::warning` is invisible" — Nightwatch alerts on exceptions only), this branch calls `Log::warning` with no `report()` and no exception — a codebase-wide Apify credential revocation or actor deprecation would silently degrade every enrichment with zero engineering alert, discoverable only by someone manually querying logs.
    - **Plain English:** When the system can't fill in a business's extra details (menu, booking links, etc.), it writes a note in a private log and quietly moves on — the individual user does see "not available" on their dashboard, but if this starts happening to EVERY user because a vendor service broke, nobody on the engineering team gets paged. A single missed delivery isn't a big deal; a whole warehouse going dark with no alarm is.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($enrichment === null && $harvest === []) {
            Log::warning('google_business.enrich_job.enrichment_unavailable', [
                'user_id' => $this->userId,
                'place_id' => $this->placeId,
                'apify_attempted' => $apifyAttempted,
                'apify_returned_null' => true,
                'apify_result_reused' => $reusedCachedResult,
                'apify_orphaned_run' => $orphanedRun,
                'harvest_empty' => true,
                'website' => $gbp->website(),
                'category' => $gbp->category(),
            ]);

            $this->mark($connection, 'unavailable');

            return;
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-JOB-3** · P2 — GoogleBusinessEnrichJob's inflight-marker orphan check can discard an already-fetched (and billed) Apify result on an unrelated exception
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php:173-196
    - **Affects:** Google Business enrichment retries when the paid Apify call actually succeeded but a subsequent step in the same attempt threw — the result is discarded and no further attempt is made within the retry window, wasting the paid Apify call.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap `Cache::put($resultKey, ...)` (and any code between the inflight-marker write and the result-cache write) so that if it throws, the marker written just before it is cleared before the exception propagates — this preserves the "refuse to re-bill after an unrecoverable worker death" policy (which relies on an *uncatchable* kill leaving no chance to clean up) while not conflating it with a *catchable* exception, where the process is demonstrably still alive and can safely undo its own bookkeeping.
        - Keep the existing behavior for a true worker kill (SIGKILL/OOM/deploy) — that scenario never reaches a `catch` block by definition, so it is unaffected by this change and correctly still treated as "orphaned, don't re-bill."
    - **Technical:** Category 1/3. Verified against `GoogleBusinessApifyScraper::fetch()` (app/Services/Platforms/GoogleBusinessApifyScraper.php:55-67): network/timeout failures are caught *inside* the scraper and return `null` rather than throwing, so the "transient network error" framing DeepSeek's draft used does not apply — `fetch()` itself essentially never throws in normal operation. The real residual risk is narrower but still real: between `Cache::put($inflightKey, ...)` (line 193) succeeding and `Cache::put($resultKey, ...)` (line 196) succeeding, any exception — a Redis blip on the result-cache write, or an unexpected error in response mapping — leaves the inflight marker set with no cached result. On retry, `$inflightBefore !== null` at line 173 is indistinguishable from a genuine worker-kill, so the job takes the "orphaned run" branch (lines 173-188), discards the enrichment (which may have already been successfully fetched and billed by Apify), and never attempts the fetch again within the 900s TTL window — silently underspending the `$maxExceptions = 2` retry budget the job was configured with.
    - **Plain English:** This job puts up a "do not disturb" sign right before making a paid phone call, planning to take it down once it's written down what the call told it. If something goes wrong writing that down — even something unrelated to the call itself, like a brief database hiccup — the sign stays up, and the next attempt sees the sign and assumes the worst (that the process died mid-call), throwing away a call that may have already succeeded and been paid for, instead of just trying again.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($inflightKey, now()->toIso8601String(), self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL);
        $apifyAttempted = true;
        $enrichment = $scraper->fetch($this->placeId, $this->userId);
        Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL);
        ```
        ```php
        } elseif ($inflightBefore !== null) {
            $enrichment = null;
            $orphanedRun = true;
            Log::warning('google_business.enrich_job.apify_orphaned_run', [
                'user_id' => $this->userId,
                'place_id' => $this->placeId,
                'inflight_since' => $inflightBefore,
            ]);
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-JOB-4** · P3 — SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob returns silently on invalid host or missing user
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/SweepPreviousWebsiteCardsJob.php:61-69
    - **Affects:** The card-cleanup sweep that retires scrape-seeded links matching a user's own previous website — a malformed URL or a stale dispatch just no-ops, leaving duplicate cards live with no signal.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - For `hostOf($this->previousWebsite) === null`, call `$this->fail(new \InvalidArgumentException(...))` — a malformed URL reaching this job indicates a caller-side bug worth surfacing.
        - For `User::find($this->userId) === null`, leave as a silent no-op if this is an expected race (dispatched, then the user record is gone) — verified via `WorkplaceObserver::dispatchCardSweep()` (app/Observers/Core/WorkplaceObserver.php:156-169), which fires from the `saved` hook after the triggering write has landed, so a missing user is more likely a genuinely deleted account than a commit race; downgrade the missing-user branch to a debug log rather than failing.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. This is a low-stakes cleanup/hygiene job (`content.items` sweep, not a correctness-critical path) — its own `failed()` handler already documents that exhausted retries leave "scrape-seeded cards ... may still be live," implying the team is aware unresolved runs leave stale state. The invalid-host early return bypasses that documented awareness entirely by never reaching `failed()`. Consequence is cosmetic (a duplicate link card lingers), not data loss or a security issue, which is why this sits at P3 rather than P2.
    - **Plain English:** This tidy-up job removes duplicate website links after someone updates their "previous website" info. If the web address it's given is broken, or the person it's cleaning up after no longer exists, it just quietly stops — no note, no error. The mess it was supposed to clean up stays there, and nobody finds out unless they go looking.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $host = $this->hostOf($this->previousWebsite);
        if ($host === null) {
            return;
        }

        $user = User::find($this->userId);
        if ($user === null) {
            return;
        }
        ```

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W1-OBS-1** · P1 — MenuAiExtractor swallows every Mistral/DeepSeek failure with `Log::warning` only — no `report()`, no `$this->fail()`
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/MenuAiExtractor.php:136-146, 187-197
    - **Affects:** All four automatic AI menu-scan paths (`GoogleMenuPhotoScanJob`, `WebsiteMenuPdfScanJob`, `WebsiteMenuHtmlScanJob`) plus the manual `MenuController::scan()` endpoint — this is, per its own docblock, "the single implementation since 2026-08-26."
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report($e)` in the two `catch (Throwable $e)` blocks (`ocr()`, `structure()`) so an actual transport exception reaches Nightwatch, matching the convention already used elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `InstagramConnectionSeeder::mirrorOne`, `MenuApifyScraper::attemptScrape`'s 5xx branch).
        - For the `! $response->successful()` branches, add `report()` at least on a sustained-failure basis (e.g. via `EscalatesRepeatedFaults`, already used by `MediaMirror`/`PoolResolver` in this codebase) so a rotated/expired API key doesn't degrade silently forever.
    - **Technical:** Unlike sibling vendor-I/O call sites in this codebase (`MenuApifyScraper::attemptScrape` calls `report()` on a 5xx; `InstagramConnectionSeeder::mirrorOne`'s catch calls `report($e)`), `MenuAiExtractor::ocr()` and `::structure()` never call `report()` on any failure path — network exception or non-2xx response alike. Because this is the sole AI menu-extraction lane, a Mistral/DeepSeek outage, a rotated API key, or a budget/quota change degrades every automatic and manual menu scan to an empty result with zero Nightwatch signal — visible only to someone actively reading Cloud logs.
    - **Plain English:** This is the only "brain" that reads a photographed menu and turns it into a real menu on the site. If the AI provider's key expires or their service goes down, the system quietly gives up and writes a note nobody is watching — every menu scan silently stops working and no one gets paged.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            Log::warning('menu_ai.ocr.threw', ['user_id' => $userId, 'error' => $e->getMessage()]);

            return null;
        }
        ```

        ```php
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            Log::warning('menu_ai.structure.threw', ['user_id' => $userId, 'error' => $e->getMessage()]);

            return null;
        }
        ```

- [x] **#W1-OBS-2** · P1 — ProjectionWriter's media-merge cap drops surplus photos with `Log::warning` only — real, unrecoverable data loss
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:1762-1771 (`foldCollections`)
    - **Affects:** Any user whose merged content items collectively carry more than `content.merge_media_cap` (default 8) images — the surplus is permanently discarded, not deferred.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report(new MediaFoldCapExceeded($userId, $keptItemId, $mediaDropped))` (or equivalent) alongside the existing `Log::warning`, following the same pattern already used elsewhere in this file (`self::escalateIfSustained`, used a few hundred lines away for `media_mirror.dispatch`).
        - Consider raising `merge_media_cap` or making it a two-stage warn/report threshold if drops turn out to be routine rather than exceptional.
    - **Technical:** The code's own comment states "A silent cap is a defect in this codebase," yet the only signal on exceeding it is `Log::warning('content.merge_fold.media_capped', …)`. Per the Nightwatch alert model, this is exactly a breadcrumb, not an alert — the projection run completes successfully and Horizon shows no failure. Unlike most other findings in this audit, this is genuine, permanent data loss on a plausible, recurring operation (an identity-resolution merge), not a transient/self-healing degradation.
    - **Plain English:** When two duplicate listings for the same user get merged into one, the system keeps a safety-limited number of photos and throws the rest away for good. It writes a note in a logbook nobody is watching. Users can lose real photos with zero warning to your team.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($mediaDropped > 0) {
            // A silent cap is a defect in this codebase. user + item so it is
            // attributable.
            Log::warning('content.merge_fold.media_capped', [
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'item_id' => $keptItemId,
                'kept' => $mediaHeld,
                'dropped' => $mediaDropped,
            ]);
        }
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): FIXED, rescoped — the "unrecoverable data loss" framing holds for the MANUAL lane ONLY. A connector coord's dropped media is re-derived by the next reprojection from ingest.record_versions via the uncapped replaceCollections() path (foldCollections docblock :1583-1586, replaceCollections :2136-2139), so for connector-sourced media the drop is transient and self-healing. The manual lane IS terminal: writeManualItem() writes facets once from an HTTP payload persisted nowhere, so there is nothing to replay, and $hasCuration only spares a loser that is pinned or has a manual_overrides row — an unpinned hand-added item with photos was discarded for good. Two further corrections: this is NOT core.enforce_site_gallery_max6 (that trigger is on site.site_media, a different table from content.item_media), and the prescribed escalateIfSustained pattern does not exist in ProjectionWriter at all — its idiom is a bare report(). Fixed by exempting manual-origin rows from the cap (joined via item_media.source_item_id, since source_items are repointed onto the survivor before the fold) and adding report(MergeFoldMediaDroppedException) beside the retained Log::warning. Manual rows still increment the held counter, so they displace connector rows rather than defeating the cap. Dev-only exposure today: production carries no content schema.

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-3** · P2 — GoogleBusinessEnrichJob caches a null Apify enrichment result, poisoning retries for 30 minutes
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php:193-224
    - **Affects:** Google Business connect users whose paid Apify enrichment returns nothing and whose website harvest is also empty — any retry (job-level or user-initiated reconnect) within `APIFY_RESULT_TTL` (1800s) reuses the cached null and skips re-scraping.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Don't cache `['enrichment' => null]` as a reusable positive result, or clear `$resultKey` (not just `$inflightKey`) in the `enrichment === null && harvest === []` soft-failure branch so a manual reconnect within the TTL gets a fresh attempt.
        - Keep the existing structured `Log::warning` (already improved per the code's own "OBS-6" annotation) — the fix here is the cache invalidation, not the logging.
    - **Technical:** `Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL)` runs unconditionally, including when `$enrichment` is null. The soft-failure branch that follows logs and marks `apify_status = 'unavailable'` but never clears `$resultKey`, so any retry — whether a job-level retry or a user re-triggering the connect from the dashboard — reads the cached null and reuses the 'unavailable' outcome for up to 30 minutes. The job's own extensive commentary shows this path was already hardened once (tagged "OBS-6") for log context; the remaining gap is the cache poisoning, not the alerting.
    - **Plain English:** When the enrichment lookup comes back empty, the system writes "empty" in a sticky note for half an hour. If the business owner tries to reconnect right away hoping for a fresh check, the system just reads the same stale "empty" note instead of trying again — self-healing only once the note expires.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
                Cache::put($inflightKey, now()->toIso8601String(), self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL);
                $apifyAttempted = true;
                $enrichment = $scraper->fetch($this->placeId, $this->userId);
                Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL);
        ```
        ```php
        if ($enrichment === null && $harvest === []) {
            Log::warning('google_business.enrich_job.enrichment_unavailable', [...]);
            $this->mark($connection, 'unavailable');

            return;
        }
        ```

If this becomes a pattern for many restaurants at once (a platform-wide scrape break), nobody on the team would know until an owner complains.

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-6** · P2 — SquareMenuDriver silently returns a partial catalog on a mid-pagination failure
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/SquareMenuDriver.php:232-237
    - **Affects:** Square stores with more than one products page (>100 items); a mid-fetch failure silently truncates the catalog.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Log a structured warning at this decision point (`page`, `products_so_far`) so a truncated catalog is distinguishable from a genuinely small one.
        - Consider surfacing a `truncated: true` flag through `fetchProducts()` so `MenuFetchJob` can avoid retiring dishes it simply didn't re-see this run (it already does this for whole-platform failures via `excludeFailedPlatformExclusives`, but not for a partial fetch).
    - **Technical:** After a `null` response mid-pagination, `return $products === [] ? null : $products;` silently keeps whatever pages succeeded — no log line records that the returned catalog is a truncated prefix, not the full menu. `productsPage()`'s own per-page attempts log misses, but nothing at this call site says the aggregate result is incomplete.
    - **Plain English:** If page 3 of a 5-page menu fails to load, the system quietly serves pages 1, 2, 4, 5 as if that were the whole menu — with no note that anything is missing. Dishes on the failed page can silently vanish from the site.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
            if ($resp === null) {
                // A transient miss on page 1 fails the whole fetch (the caller
                // ghosts the platform and retirement is deferred); a miss
                // mid-pagination keeps what arrived rather than losing the
                // store over one page.
                return $products === [] ? null : $products;
            }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-7** · P2 — DerivedDescriptorFactory silently returns an empty descriptor set when the compiled catalog is missing
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/Registry/DerivedDescriptorFactory.php:102-110
    - **Affects:** Platform-registry boot on every request. Per the 2026-08-27 PD-retirement programme (commits `e2daebde0`, `a3f0a8d32`: "every brand platform catalog-derived; provider reduced to shop + derivation"), the compiled catalog is now the *sole* source for essentially every brand platform's descriptor — not a minority of them as when this fallback was first written.

    - **What to do:**

        - Given the blast radius (post `a3f0a8d32`, this affects nearly every brand platform's connect/route availability), consider a deploy-time or boot-time assertion that the catalog artefact exists, rather than relying purely on request-time detection.

This was a low-blast-radius fallback when written (a handful of derived descriptors), but the same-day P5/P6 teardown commits moved every remaining hand-written brand descriptor (including Instagram, the last one) onto this same derivation path via `BEHAVIOUR_BINDINGS`. A missing or corrupted compiled-catalog artefact in production now silently drops the connect routes for nearly the entire platform registry, with zero signal beyond "users can't find the connect button."
    - **Plain English:** Almost every "connect your Instagram / Spotify / Google Business" button on the dashboard is now built from one master catalog file at boot time. If that file is missing or broken during a deploy, the app starts up fine and just quietly loses most of those connect buttons — no alarm, no error, just a dashboard that looks like half its features vanished.
    - **Evidence:**

        ```php
        } catch (CatalogNotCompiled) {

            // any bootstrap that runs before catalog:compile depend on it.
            return [];
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-10** · P2 — ProjectionWriter's media-mirror dispatch skip/cap summary is logged at `info`, which production's `LOG_LEVEL` drops
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:2904-2916
    - **Affects:** Owned media assets that hit `mirror_attempts >= max` or other skip paths — they stay on external hotlinked URLs indefinitely with no CDN copy.

    - **Technical:** The code's own comment acknowledges "prod's LOG_LEVEL defaults to `warning` and would drop it" and states the durable record is the `mirror_attempts` row, not this log line — a reasonable position for the *row*, but there is still no Nightwatch-visible signal when a batch of assets permanently exhausts its mirror budget. `Log::info` here is genuinely unreachable in production by design.
    - **Plain English:** The system copies user photos into its own storage so the site doesn't depend on outside websites staying up. If a batch of photos permanently fails to copy, the only note about it is written in a debug-level channel that production doesn't even keep. Nobody would notice a wave of failed copies until users report broken images.
    - **Evidence:**

        ```php
        ]);
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-11** · P2 — ProjectionWriter's identity-scope cap fallback is `Log::warning` only — chronic latency invisible to Nightwatch
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:1029-1043
    - **Affects:** Projection run latency/DB load for users with large catalogues whose identity-scope narrowing cap is routinely exceeded.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Emit a `report()`/metric when `$capped` is true, in addition to the existing `Log::warning`, so a sustained fallback-to-whole-kind pattern is Nightwatch-visible rather than only discoverable by log search.
    - **Technical:** `$capped` means the identity-scope walk aborted and fell back to resolving the entire kind — exactly the expensive path the cap exists to avoid. The code's own comment says "NOT silent: an invisible fallback reads as 'narrowing works' while every run pays the full cost" — but under this codebase's own Nightwatch alert model, a `Log::warning` with no `report()` *is* silent to on-call, contradicting the comment's stated intent.
    - **Plain English:** There's a shortcut meant to check only a small slice of a big user's content instead of everything. When a user's content is too tangled for the shortcut, the system gives up and checks everything — which is much slower. It writes "gave up, did it the slow way" in a logbook, but that logbook isn't hooked up to any alarm, so a slowdown that becomes routine for big accounts goes unnoticed.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($capped) {
            // NOT silent: an invisible fallback reads as "narrowing works" while
            // every run pays the full cost. user + kind so it is attributable.
            Log::warning('identity scope cap hit — resolving whole kind', [
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'kind' => $kind,
                'touched_count' => count($touchedCoords),
                'resolving_count' => count($sourceItems),
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-12** · P2 — ComputeContentPopularityScores swallows `QueryException` across five methods, conflating test-env absence with real prod failures
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:288-291, 383-385, 538-542, 705-716, 748-751
    - **Affects:** Site-scoring/ranking freshness for content-heavy sites; operators diagnosing stale rankings.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Replace the broad `QueryException` catches with an explicit `Schema::hasTable()` check where "table absent" is a legitimate partial-env state.
        - For any other `QueryException`, call `report($e)` so a real production query fault (not a missing table) reaches Nightwatch instead of silently narrowing the scoring sweep.
    - **Technical:** Five separate catch blocks (`readWatermark`, `siteIdsWithRecentEvents`, `servedPools`, `itemKinds`, `itemIdAliases`) all treat any `QueryException` as "table absent, pre-migration env" and continue with a degraded/empty result. In production, where these tables exist, a genuine transient query fault (lock timeout, connection blip) is swallowed the same way, silently narrowing the scoring sweep for that site with zero trace beyond the fact that its rankings look stale later.
    - **Plain English:** This job computes what content is popular on each site. If part of the database it needs to read has a hiccup, the job just quietly skips that part and moves on as if nothing happened. Weeks later someone notices rankings look wrong, with no record of why.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (QueryException) {
            return null; // table absent (pre-migration env) — fixed window.
        }
        ```
        ```php
        } catch (QueryException) {
            // content.* lane absent — traffic scope only.
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-13** · P3 — CustomLinkSeeder swallows URL-canonicalization exceptions with a fully empty catch
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/CustomLinkSeeder.php:92-96

    - **Plain English:** If the URL clean-up step breaks, links just get filed messier than usual — no one is told. Worst case, a link appears twice on someone's page.

    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/FreshaScraper.php:132-136
    - **Affects:** Fresha slug-rotation recovery. A failed probe here is masked by the caller: `fetchLocation()` still attempts the (already-known-dead) canonical page and surfaces a visible `abort(502, ...)` regardless, so the overall failure is not silent — only the specific root cause is undiagnosable.

    - **Technical:** The catch discards the `Throwable` and returns null; `fetchLocation()`'s caller still hits its own `abort(502, ...)` for the already-failing canonical page, so this failure does surface as an exception up the stack — it just can't be distinguished from "Fresha genuinely doesn't have a redirect for this slug" without a log line here.
    - **Plain English:** When the system tries to check whether a salon's booking-page address changed, and that check itself fails, the overall page load still visibly fails — just without a note explaining that the *address check* was the specific thing that broke.

        ```php
        } catch (Throwable) {

        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-15** · P3 — MenuApifyScraper treats a 4xx Apify response as `Log::info` only, deliberately without `report()`
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/MenuApifyScraper.php:585-599
    - **Affects:** Menu scrape pipeline. A sustained account-wide issue (actor unrented, billing paused) would produce only info-level logs.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Consider escalating (via `EscalatesRepeatedFaults`, already used elsewhere in this codebase) when 4xx responses recur across many distinct stores in a short window — a signal of an account-level problem rather than a per-store one.
    - **Technical:** This is a deliberate, commented design choice ("5xx is genuine Apify infra worth alerting on; 4xx… is a hard error") distinguishing infra failures from per-store misses, and it is duplicative of OBS-4/OBS-5's broader "total scrape failure invisible" finding when it manifests account-wide. Kept as a low-tier residual risk rather than dropped, since a billing lapse is a plausible single point of failure this design doesn't cover.
    - **Plain English:** If the scraping vendor blocks an entire ordering account (e.g. a billing issue), every affected store's menu fetch fails the same way an ordinary "store doesn't exist" would — quietly, with a low-priority note only.

        ```php
                'platform' => $platform,
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'attempt' => $attempt,
                'status' => $response->status(),

        }
        ```

        ```php
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'kind' => $kind,
                'capped_key_count' => count($resolution->cappedKeys),
                'sample' => array_slice($resolution->cappedKeys, 0, 5, preserve_keys: true),
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-17** · P3 — LinkProjector's malformed-detector reporting is silently disabled if the report call itself throws
    - **Where:** app/Routing/LinkProjector.php:288-312 (`reportMalformedPattern`)
    - **Affects:** On-call visibility of a bad routing-detector pattern, but only during a compound failure (cache/Redis outage coinciding with a malformed pattern hit).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Log the observability-failure itself in the catch (e.g. `Log::warning('routing.malformed_pattern_report_failed', ['detector_id' => $detectorId, 'error' => $e->getMessage()])`) without rethrowing.
    - **Technical:** The whole throttle/log/report sequence sits inside one `try` with an empty `catch (Throwable)`. If `Cache::add()` throws (Redis unavailable), neither the `Log::error` nor the `report()` runs. This is a narrow, low-probability compound failure — a Redis/cache outage of this severity would independently trigger many other alerts across the platform (session checks, queue, rate limiting all share the same Redis instance per this repo's architecture), making this specific gap a minor residual rather than the primary signal of such an outage.
    - **Plain English:** The alarm for "a link-recognition rule is broken" is wired through the same system as the cache. If the cache has a serious outage, this one specific alarm goes dark too — but a cache outage that severe would already be triggering plenty of other alarms elsewhere.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        try {
            $ttl = max(1, (int) config('partna.routing.malformed_pattern_report_ttl_seconds', 3600));
            if (! Cache::add("routing:malformed-detector:{$detectorId}:{$field}", 1, $ttl)) {
                return;
            }
            Log::error('routing.detector.malformed_pattern', [...]);
            report(new MalformedDetectorPatternException($detectorId, $field, $pattern));
        } catch (Throwable) {
            // Observability must never become the fault. A cache outage costs
            // the signal, never the verdict — and never a 500 on the preview.
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-18** · P3 — PoolResolver's ingest-badge read-failure warning lacks site_id/connection context
    - **Where:** app/Site/Pools/PoolResolver.php:872-883
    - **Affects:** Cloud-log searchability during public sitepage pool-hydration failures.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Add `'site_id' => $site->id` (already in scope) to the warning.
    - **Technical:** This is pure log-searchability hygiene — the code already calls `self::escalateIfSustained($e, 'pool_ingest_badges')` right after, so a sustained failure IS Nightwatch-visible; the only gap is that the individual breadcrumb can't be filtered by site during triage.
    - **Plain English:** When the system can't read sync badges for a sitepage, it correctly pages someone if it keeps happening — it just doesn't say which customer's site was affected in the meantime, making triage slower than it needs to be.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
            } catch (QueryException $e) {
                Log::warning('pools.ingest_badges_read_failed', ['error' => $e->getMessage()]);
                self::escalateIfSustained($e, 'pool_ingest_badges');
                $ingestByConnection = [];
            }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-19** · P3 — InstagramMediaUrl logs an Apify actor exception at `info` instead of `warning`
    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/InstagramMediaUrl.php:170-174
    - **Affects:** Triage speed only — a real vendor-call exception is logged at the same severity as routine successful-refresh chatter.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Change to `Log::warning('media_mirror.refresh_actor_failed', [...])`.
    - **Technical:** The caller intentionally falls back to poster-only regardless, so this isn't a missed alert by itself — just a severity mismatch that makes a genuine upstream exception invisible to anyone filtering Cloud logs by warning/error during an incident.
    - **Plain English:** A failed vendor call gets logged with the same "nothing to see here" priority as a success. Someone scanning only for warnings during an incident would miss it entirely.
    - **Evidence:**

        ```php
        } catch (\Throwable $e) {
            Log::info('media_mirror.refresh_actor_failed', ['short_code' => $shortCode, 'error' => $e->getMessage()]);

            return null;
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-20** · P3 — InstagramMediaUrl silently disables the Apify actor refresh leg when the token is unset
    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/InstagramMediaUrl.php:149-152
    - **Affects:** Carousel-image refreshes and any embed-leg-failure fallback for Instagram media mirroring.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Emit `Log::error` (or add to boot-time config validation) when `services.apify.token` is empty and this method is reached, so the gap is diagnosable rather than inferred from downstream symptoms.
    - **Technical:** A missing token silently disables only the actor-refresh fallback within this one method; the embed-page leg still works for videos and first-carousel-images, so this is a narrow degradation, not a full outage — and a genuinely missing Apify token would independently break several other Apify-dependent flows in this codebase (`MenuApifyScraper`, `GoogleBusinessEnrichJob`) that already have their own logging, making a total token-outage likely visible elsewhere first.
    - **Plain English:** One missing API credential quietly turns off the backup restocking path for deeper Instagram carousel photos. It's a narrow effect since the credential is shared by several bigger features that would likely surface the problem first.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php

        if (! $token) {
            return null;
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-21** · P3 — ComputeContentPopularityScores::writeWatermark failure is `Log::warning` only
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:300-303
    - **Affects:** Scoring-scheduler efficiency only. On failure, the run falls back to a fixed 60-minute lookback window instead of the persisted watermark — self-healing every run, never breaking a feature.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Add `report($e)` if this proves to fail persistently in practice (e.g. via a sustained-failure counter); otherwise the existing comment ("Never fail the sweep over its bookkeeping row — but say so") already captures the intended tradeoff correctly.
    - **Technical:** A persistently broken `analytics.scoring_watermarks` table would cause every 15-minute run to fall back to the wider fixed window (more recompute, not less coverage) — a performance cost, not a correctness or data-loss issue, so this sits at the bottom of this audit's severity range.
    - **Plain English:** If the job's own bookmark can't be saved, it just re-reads a bit more history each time instead of picking up exactly where it left off — slower, but nothing breaks and nothing is lost.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (QueryException $e) {
            // Never fail the sweep over its bookkeeping row — but say so.
            Log::warning('analytics.scoring_watermark_write_failed', ['error' => $e->getMessage()]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-OBS-22** · P3 — BackfillStorefrontFaviconsCommand's summary line hides skipped-candidate counts
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/BackfillStorefrontFaviconsCommand.php:60-92
    - **Affects:** The operator running this manual, on-demand maintenance command (`shop:backfill-favicons`) — not an automated/scheduled path.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:** Track and report separate `$skippedStore`/`$skippedSource` counters in the final summary line.
    - **Technical:** The loop silently `continue`s when `$store === null` or the source URL is empty, but the final `$this->info(...)` only reports `$missed` (favicon fetch attempted-and-failed). A run can report "still without an icon 0" while having silently skipped many candidates it never attempted, giving the operator false confidence in coverage. Low severity because this is a manually-invoked maintenance command, not an automated pipeline.
    - **Plain English:** This cleanup tool can say "all done, nothing missing" while it actually skipped a bunch of stores it couldn't even attempt — giving whoever runs it a false sense that the job is complete.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($store === null) {
            continue;
        }

        $source = $store->url ?: $store->sourceUrl;
        if (! is_string($source) || trim($source) === '') {
            continue;
        }
        ```
        ```php
        $this->info("Candidates {$candidates->count()}; {$verb} {$found}; still without an icon {$missed}.");
        ```

## Suggested Bundled Sessions

    - **Why grouped:** Same MenuFetchJob → MenuApifyScraper → per-platform-driver pipeline; the same "log-only, no report()" contract needs to land consistently across all three files in one pass.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 2 — Instagram media-refresh observability:** OBS-8, OBS-9, OBS-19, OBS-20
    - **Why grouped:** Same Instagram media pipeline (`InstagramConnectionSeeder` ↔ `InstagramMediaUrl`), same freshUrl()/mirrorOne() call graph, same fix shape (add/upgrade a log line).
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 3 — ProjectionWriter cap/skip visibility:** OBS-2, OBS-10, OBS-11, OBS-16
    - **Why grouped:** Same file, same recurring "cap hit → Log::warning only" pattern; one pass can add a consistent `report()`/metric convention across all four call sites.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet. (OBS-2 is real data loss — implementer should confirm the fix doesn't change fold semantics, just adds the alert.)

- **Bundle 4 — Analytics scoring command observability:** OBS-12, OBS-21
    - **Why grouped:** Same file (`ComputeContentPopularityScores`), same `QueryException`/bookkeeping-log pattern.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 5 — Low-stakes silent catches:** OBS-13, OBS-14, OBS-17, OBS-18, OBS-22
    - **Why grouped:** Small, independent, single-file S-effort fixes (add a log line / add context) with no shared subsystem — batching them into one sweep session avoids five separate plan/review cycles for trivial changes.

## Standalone — do NOT bundle

- **OBS-1 — MenuAiExtractor swallows AI-provider failures** · P1, sole implementation of a vendor-integration lane feeding four call sites (three jobs + one controller endpoint) — the fix needs a deliberate decision about report()-vs-fail() semantics across both public methods, not a mechanical log-line add.
- **OBS-7 — DerivedDescriptorFactory silent empty-catalog fallback** · Touches the platform-registry boot path, which as of the same-day P5/P6 teardown commits (`e2daebde0`, `a3f0a8d32`) is now foundational to nearly every brand platform's connect route. The blast radius and recency of the underlying architecture shift warrant an isolated plan and sign-off rather than folding into a batch.

## P2 — Should fix

- [x] **#W1-CCG-2** · P2 — Uncached dashboard read: menu pin-order join recomputed on every dashboard compose
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/MenuPayloadComposer.php:250–261 (`pinOrder()` method)
    - **Affects:** Every authenticated owner loading the dashboard menu payload (`GET /platforms/menu` and post-write echoes); Supabase PostgreSQL primary under concurrent requests.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Wrap `pinOrder($userId)` in `CacheLockService::rememberLocked` with a `CacheKeyGenerator` key scoped to `(string) $userId`.
        - Use a bounded TTL (60–300s, aligned to how often owners reorder menus in practice) with jitter.
        - Invalidate the key whenever the owner reorders menu items (dish drag-and-drop on the pool page), by hooking into the section-item write seam (`site.section_items.sort_key` mutations).
    - **Technical:** Category 1 — uncached hot dashboard read. `MenuPayloadComposer::dashboardPayload()` (line 48) → `compose()` (line 118) → `categories()` (line 149) → `contentCategories()` (line 186) → `pinOrder()` (line 210). The `pinOrder()` method (lines 250–261) runs a three-table join across `site.section_items`, `site.sections`, and `site.sites`, filtered by user ID, menu section key, and pin state. It extracts the owner's current menu item order from `site.section_items.sort_key`. This join produces the same logical result until the owner reorders the menu — which is a rare event compared to how many times the menu dashboard is viewed. Each dashboard load (or write echo) recomputes this exact join without a cache. A user-scoped TTL cache with push invalidation on menu reorder would remove repeated join work from a frequently-accessed authenticated endpoint.
    - **Plain English:** Every time an owner opens their menu dashboard or creates/edits a dish, the backend re-joins three database tables to figure out what order the owner currently has their menu items in — even though that order almost never changes between views. It's like recalculating a seating chart every time someone glances at the restaurant instead of keeping a printed version at the host stand and only updating it when someone actually moves a table.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // MenuPayloadComposer::dashboardPayload() calls compose():
        public function dashboardPayload(User $user): array
        {
            $menu = $this->load($user);
            // ...
            return $this->compose($user, $menu);
        }

        // compose() calls categories():
        public function compose(User $user, ?Menu $menu): array
        {
            return [
                // ...
                'categories' => $this->categories($user, $menu),
                // ...
            ];
        }

        // categories() calls contentCategories():
        private function categories(User $user, ?Menu $menu): array
        {
            // ...
            return $this->contentCategories((string) $user->id, $rows);
        }

        // contentCategories() calls pinOrder():
        private function contentCategories(string $userId, Collection $rows): array
        {
            // ...
            $pins = $this->pinOrder($userId);
            // ...
        }

        // pinOrder() runs the uncached three-table join:
        private function pinOrder(string $userId): array
        {
            return DB::connection('pgsql')->table('site.section_items as si')
                ->join('site.sections as s', 's.id', '=', 'si.section_id')
                ->join('site.sites as st', 'st.id', '=', 's.site_id')
                ->where('st.user_id', $userId)
                ->where('s.key', PoolRegistry::sectionKey('menus'))
                ->where('si.state', SectionItem::STATE_PINNED)
                ->whereNotNull('si.sort_key')
                ->pluck('si.sort_key', 'si.item_id')
                ->map(fn ($sortKey) => (float) $sortKey)
                ->all();
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [x] **#W1-CCG-1** · P3 — Sitepage build queries the same design-singleton media twice per request
    - **Where:** app/Services/PublicSite/IndividualProfilePayloadBuilder.php:328 and 359 (methods `buildBrand()` and `buildHeadshot()`)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage cache-miss rebuilds (every request that needs to assemble the profile payload after an invalidation or cold start).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Resolve the design singletons map once in `IndividualProfilePayloadBuilder::build()` before calling `buildBrand()` and `buildHeadshot()`.
        - Pass the already-loaded map into both methods, or memoize the result on the resolver instance with request-scoped lifetime (using a private property or static memo).
    - **Technical:** Category 4 — repeated identical read within one request. On the public sitepage payload path, `build()` (line 90) calls `buildBrand($site)` (line 131), which invokes `$this->resolver->getDesignSingletons($site)` (line 328), and then `buildHeadshot($site)` (line 135), which invokes the same method again (line 359). `getDesignSingletons()` (SitepageDataResolverService:670) runs a multi-row `SiteMedia` query filtered by site ID, pool, purpose, active state, processing state, and eager-loads `mediaVariants` with per-variant URL resolution. The same logical collection is computed twice in the same request lifecycle with no per-request reason to differ. Canonical fix is request-scoped memoization — a private property or the resolver's own request-scoped memo — not a Redis cache (the value is specific to one request).
    - **Plain English:** When assembling a public page, we ask the filing cabinet for the same folder of logo/image files twice in a row — once to get the logos, once to get the headshot. The folder hasn't changed between those two asks. We should ask once and reuse the folder for both parts of the assembly.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // IndividualProfilePayloadBuilder::build() calls both:
        'brand' => $this->buildBrand($site),
        // ... line 131
        'headshot' => $this->buildHeadshot($site),
        // ... line 135

        // buildBrand() fetches:
        private function buildBrand(?Site $site): array
        {
            $singletons = $this->resolver->getDesignSingletons($site);
            // ... line 328
        }

        // buildHeadshot() fetches the same:
        private function buildHeadshot(?Site $site): ?array
        {
            $row = $this->resolver->getDesignSingletons($site)[SiteMedia::PURPOSE_HEADSHOT] ?? null;
            // ... line 359
        }

        // getDesignSingletons() runs the expensive read:
        public function getDesignSingletons(?Site $site): array
        {
            return SiteMedia::query()
                ->where('site_id', $site->id)
                ->where('pool', SiteMedia::POOL_DESIGN)
                ->whereIn('purpose', SiteMedia::designSingletonPurposes())
                ->where('is_active', true)
                ->where('processing_state', SiteMedia::PROCESSING_STATE_READY)
                ->with('mediaVariants')
                ->get()
                // ... maps variant URLs
        }
        ```

- [x] **#W1-CCG-3** · P3 — Product-intent link preview fetches the same URL twice when product markup is absent
    - **Where:** app/Http/Controllers/Api/Content/LinkPreviewController.php:67–102 (show method, product intent fallback path)
    - **Affects:** Dashboard Add-link flow when users paste a product page without product markup; vendor rate-limit exposure and preview latency.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Fetch the page once per request and pass the raw body/headers to both `GenericShopScraper::readProductPage()` and `LinkCardScraper::snapshotOrMinimal()` parsers instead of making two separate fetches.
        - Alternatively, memoize the outbound fetch result per normalized URL for the request lifecycle so both parsing paths share one response.
    - **Technical:** Category 4 — repeated identical read within one request. For `intent=product` (line 67), `show()` first opens a fetch budget into `GenericShopScraper::readProductPage($url)` (line 69). If no product markup is found (line 96 fallthrough), it immediately calls `LinkCardScraper::snapshotOrMinimal($url)` (line 102), issuing a second synchronous outbound HTTP fetch of the same URL. Both are expensive vendor calls via `SafeUrlFetcher`. The read is recomputed within one request lifecycle. Request-scoped memoization (either pass the response between callers or cache it per-request) is the canonical fix; a Redis cache would not fully solve this because the two parse paths are separate calls in the same request.
    - **Plain English:** When someone pastes a product link, we phone the website once to ask "is this a product?" and, if we're not sure, we immediately phone the same website again to ask "well, what is this then?" We should keep the first answer and reuse it for the second question instead of making two calls.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // POST /api/content/links/preview with intent=product
        if (($data['intent'] ?? 'link') === 'product') {
            // First fetch
            $seconds = (float) config('partna.http_fetch.connect_budget_seconds', 20);
            $read = $this->budget->open($seconds, fn () => $this->shop->readProductPage($url));
            // ... line 69
            if ($product !== null) {
                // Found product, return early
                return $this->success([...]);
            }
            // No product markup: fall through
        }

        // Second fetch for the same URL (line 102)
        $card = $this->scraper->snapshotOrMinimal($url);
        ```

## P0 — Must fix before any real user touches the system

- [x] **#W1-PRIV-1** · P0 — Early-access DSAR export can hand a user a stranger's job/industry/status data when an email address is recycled
    - **Resolved:** Fixed 2026-08-28 (`audit-fix/campaign-p0-2026-08-28`). NOTE the finding's prescription was stale: `core.early_access_signups.user_id` (FK to `core.users`, ON DELETE SET NULL, partial UNIQUE) has existed since the 2026-07-26 baseline on BOTH dev and prod, so no schema change was required and the L sizing was inflated. Closed by a three-bucket ownership rule in `streamEarlyAccessSignups()`: owned (`user_id`) exports in full; email-matched `waitlist` exports in full (safe because `signupFromMarketing()` refreshes content while waitlist); email-matched past-waitlist exports the row with `type`/`workplace_or_industry`/`platforms`/`status`/`invited_at`/`signed_up_at` replaced by `[withheld: ownership unverified]`; rows owned by another user are excluded. The predicate is a UNION, not a preference order — every linked dev row points at a provisional user with `primary_email IS NULL`, so a `user_id`-first fallback would have caused Article 15 UNDER-disclosure.
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:1206-1225 (`streamEarlyAccessSignups`); app/Services/EarlyAccess/EarlyAccessService.php:29-93 (`signupFromMarketing`)
    - **Affects:** Any Partna user whose email address was previously used by a *different* person to join the early-access waitlist and who was later `invited` or `signed_up`. That person's export (a legal Article 15 / APP 12 access request) will contain a stranger's `type`, `workplace_or_industry`, `platforms`, `status`, `invited_at` and `signed_up_at`.
    - **Effort:** L (~1–2d) — requires a schema change plus service-layer logic, not a one-line patch.
    - **What to do:**
        - Add a nullable ownership column to `core.early_access_signups` (e.g. `claimed_by_user_id`, set the moment a signup converts to a real `core.users` row) via a new file in `supabase/migrations/` — never a Laravel migration.
        - Change `streamEarlyAccessSignups()` to prefer that FK when present, and to only fall back to the bare `email_lc` match for rows that never progressed past `waitlist` (the case where cross-tenant risk is near-zero because nothing has been disclosed to the applicant yet).
        - Until the FK ships, at minimum strip `workplace_or_industry`/`type`/`status`/`invited_at`/`signed_up_at` from the export for any row whose `email_lc` match is the *only* link and whose status is not `waitlist`, replacing them with a documented `[withheld: ownership unverified]` marker (mirrors the `DsarPayloadFilter::WITHHELD_DISCLOSURE` pattern already used elsewhere in this same builder).
    - **Technical:** `streamEarlyAccessSignups()` looks up `core.early_access_signups` purely by `email_lc`, with no ownership FK to `core.users`. `EarlyAccessService::signupFromMarketing()` upserts via `firstOrCreate(['email_lc' => $emailLc], [...])`, which — being `firstOrCreate` — never applies the value array to an already-existing row; a repeat submission only refreshes the row's fields while `status === STATUS_WAITLIST` (confirmed at `EarlyAccessService.php:92-93`). Once a row has progressed to `invited` or `signed_up`, the row is permanently frozen to whoever filed it first. If that email is later reassigned to a different real person (a returned/reissued corporate mailbox, an abandoned personal address later re-registered with a provider) and that person creates a Partna account and later files a DSAR, `DataExportPayloadBuilder` will silently attach the *former* occupant's occupation, marketing-funnel status and timestamps to the new owner's export. This is precisely the lens's own P0 criterion: an export that can include another tenant's data. The code already documents this itself as an open, larger-than-average leak with "no schema-level fix yet" (see evidence) — it is a known, unresolved gap, not a hypothetical.
    - **Plain English:** Imagine a waiting list that only ever wrote down email addresses, never names tied to an account. Years later, somebody else legitimately starts using that same email address, joins Partna properly, and asks "show me everything you know about me" — a legal right they have. Because the waiting-list record was never re-linked to a real account, our system hands them the *previous* owner's job title, industry and sign-up history along with their own file. That is us disclosing one person's private information to a completely different person, which is exactly the kind of privacy breach the Privacy Act exists to prevent.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // Note on email-recycle: ... Once a row has progressed to invited or
        // signed_up, a resubmission under a recycled email is a silent no-op —
        // the row keeps the FORMER occupant's type, status, invited_at, and
        // signed_up_at, not just created_at/id. Larger leak than the
        // streamEmailSubscriptions case; no schema-level fix yet.
        private function streamEarlyAccessSignups(?string $email): Generator
        {
            $emailLc = $this->normaliseEmail($email);
            if ($emailLc === null) { yield from []; return; }

            yield from $this->lazyRows(
                DB::connection('pgsql')
                    ->table('core.early_access_signups')
                    ->select([...])
                    ->where('email_lc', $emailLc)
            );
        }
        ```

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W1-PRIV-2** · P1 — `content.f_review.staff_name` was added to the schema but `DataExportPayloadBuilder` never learned to export it
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:569-578 (`streamContentFReview`); supabase/migrations/20260828030000_f_review_staff_name.sql
    - **Affects:** Any professional whose site has a Fresha (or future) review connection carrying structured staff attribution — their own name, per the migration's own comment — is now stored but not returned in their own DSAR export.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `content.f_review.staff_name` to the `->select([...])` list in `streamContentFReview()`.
        - Add a one-line note to the method's docblock alongside the existing `author_name`/`author_photo_url`/`author_uri`/`text` omission list, clarifying `staff_name` is the *professional's own* identity (per the migration comment) and therefore belongs in the export, not the withheld set.
    - **Technical:** Migration `20260828030000_f_review_staff_name.sql` (2026-08-28, same day as this audit) added `content.f_review.staff_name text NULL`, explicitly documenting it as "the professional's OWN name (never reviewer PII)" — `FreshaReviewProjector` (`FreshaReviewProjector.php:60`) has emitted this value since T23b, and the column exists specifically so `ProjectionWriter` stops silently dropping it. `streamContentFReview()`'s explicit `->select()` allowlist was not updated to include it, so this direct identifier is now stored but absent from Article 15 exports. `content.f_review` is not backed by an Eloquent model, so `DataExportCoverageTest`'s model-driven PII sweep is structurally blind to this column (same blind spot the file's own docblock calls out for `moderation.evidence`'s JSON payload), and the schema-column-existence guard (`DataExportCoverageTest` T2) only checks that *selected* columns are real — it does not check that all real columns are selected. No automated test will catch this gap.
    - **Plain English:** We just added a new "drawer" to the reviews filing cabinet that stores the professional's own name, but we forgot to update the checklist the "give me my data" report reads from. So right now, if a professional asks for a copy of everything Partna holds about them, this one field quietly gets left out — a small but real gap in honouring that request completely.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        -- Person-scoped reviews (owner, 2026-08-28): persist Fresha's structured
        -- staff attribution. FreshaReviewProjector has emitted `staff_name` since
        -- T23b, but ProjectionWriter's column list never carried it, so the value was
        -- silently dropped at write time. It is the professional's OWN name (never
        -- reviewer PII), and the partna reviews scope matches on it.
        ALTER TABLE "content"."f_review" ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "staff_name" text NULL;
        ```
        ```php
        private function streamContentFReview(string $userId): Generator
        {
            return $this->lazyRows(
                DB::connection('pgsql')
                    ->table('content.f_review')
                    ->join('content.items', 'content.f_review.item_id', '=', 'content.items.id')
                    ->where('content.items.user_id', $userId)
                    ->select(['content.f_review.item_id', 'content.f_review.source_id', 'content.f_review.rating', 'content.f_review.reviewed_at', 'content.f_review.updated_at'])
            );
        }
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): FIXED, but NOT as prescribed — the finding's premise about the column is wrong. It asserts staff_name is "the professional's own name, never reviewer PII", quoting the migration COMMENT (an Evidence block that is a comment, not executable code — automatically suspect). That holds only for employee-scoped Fresha connections: for a STOREWIDE venue source, f_review rows under this user's items routinely carry a CO-WORKER's name. PoolResolver's person-scope filter (:1456-1470) exists precisely for this, and ReviewsPoolTest:530-538 pins it — owner "Simon Doyle", row reading "Jack", dropped as not his. An unconditional select would therefore have pushed a third party's name into the requester's DSAR bundle: the exact class of data this method deliberately withholds (author_name, author_photo_url, author_uri, text), and a GDPR Art. 15(4) problem in its own right. Implemented instead: select it, disclose on match, mask otherwise with a stated marker, and disclose the withholding in metadata.withheld. The matcher was extracted to PersonNameMatch so the DSAR lane and the public pool path cannot drift; equivalence was verified by a 1985-case differential harness against the pre-extraction code, 0 divergences, with ReviewsPoolTest unchanged and green as the live-path proof. Fails CLOSED when the account has no usable name. No migration.

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W1-PRIV-3** · P2 — Email-subscription export can carry a prior email-occupant's row id and timestamp on email recycle
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:1148-1191 (`streamEmailSubscriptions`)
    - **Affects:** A user whose email address was previously used to subscribe to another professional's newsletter; their export may include that prior subscription's `id` and `created_at` (no other prior-occupant fields survive).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - When the write path detects a recycled email (row content overwritten for a new subscriber), also refresh `id`/`created_at` rather than preserving the old row's identifiers, or
        - Document the residual risk explicitly in the export builder's docblock as an accepted, bounded exception (metadata-only, no schema fix planned), matching the existing candour already given to the early-access case above.
    - **Technical:** `streamEmailSubscriptions()` deliberately includes cross-tenant rows matched on `email_lc` (by design — a user subscribing to another professional's public newsletter form is that user's own data). Its own comment confirms the write path re-keys by `(list_key, email_lc)` and overwrites row *content* with the new subscriber's data, but `id` and `created_at` survive from whichever occupant created the row first. This is a materially smaller leak than #PRIV-1 (no occupation, status, or engagement history — just a UUID and a timestamp), so it does not warrant the P0/P1 tier, but it is the same root-cause pattern (email-keyed identity with no owner FK) and should be tracked and fixed the same way if #PRIV-1's FK is added.
    - **Plain English:** If you subscribe to a professional's newsletter using an email address that used to belong to someone else, your data export might include a receipt number and a signup date that actually belonged to that other person. It's a small breadcrumb — not their name or email — but it's still not yours, and shouldn't be in your file.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // Note on email-recycle: writers upsert by (list_key, email_lc), so when
        // an email is recycled (rare today) the row CONTENT is overwritten with
        // the new user's data; only created_at and id are preserved from the
        // prior occupant. ... Schema-level fix is to add an owner FK; that's a
        // follow-up.
        ->where(function ($q) use ($userId, $emailLc) {
            $q->where('user_id', $userId);
            if ($emailLc !== null) {
                $q->orWhere(function ($q2) use ($userId, $emailLc) {
                    $q2->where('email_lc', $emailLc)
                        ->where(function ($q3) use ($userId) {
                            $q3->whereNull('user_id')
                                ->orWhere('user_id', '!=', $userId);
                        });
                });
            }
        })
        ```

- [ ] **#W1-PRIV-4** · P2 — `fleet:verify` prints a site owner's contact-form email in full to console output
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/FleetVerifyCommand.php:52-55, 67-79
    - **Affects:** Account holders' notification/contact email addresses; anyone with visibility into terminal history, screen-shares, or captured `cloud command:run` output when this diagnostic is run.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Print only the on/off state for contact routing (`on`/`off`), or mask the local part (e.g. `j***@example.com`).
        - If full visibility is genuinely needed for a specific investigation, fetch it separately through an existing authenticated/audited path rather than a bare diagnostic table.
    - **Technical:** `FleetVerifyCommand::handle()` reads `notification_email` out of a block's JSONB `settings` and appends it verbatim into a `$this->table()` row rendered to the console (`on:jane@example.com`). This is a read-only staff/dev diagnostic (per its own docblock, run via `cloud command:run`), but the task it performs — confirming contact-form routing is enabled — only needs a boolean, not the underlying address. Console output sits outside the append-only `audit` schema and outside the request-boundary log-hygiene sweep, so printing the full value here is unnecessary internal over-collection of a kind the minimisation principle exists to prevent, even though the exposed value belongs to the account holder rather than a third party.
    - **Plain English:** This is a diagnostic tool staff use to check "is this business's contact form turned on?" Right now, instead of just answering yes or no, it also prints the person's full private email address to the screen. The tool only needed the yes/no answer — showing the actual address is an unnecessary extra exposure if that screen or terminal log is ever shared, saved, or seen by someone who didn't need to see it.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $email = (string) data_get($block->settings, 'notification_email', '');
        $contact = ($block->is_enabled ? 'on' : 'off').($email !== '' ? ':'.$email : '');
        // ...
        $rows[] = [
            $handle,
            (string) $user->status,
            $build->build_state ?? '-',
            $build->failure_code ?? '-',
            $headshot,
            $workplace,
            $contact,
            $http,
        ];

        $this->table(['handle', 'status', 'build', 'failure', 'headshot', 'workplace', 'contact', 'http'], $rows);
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W1-PRIV-5** · P3 — Feedback retention scheduler comment claims a 365-day default that contradicts the actual 90-day config default
    - **Where:** routes/console.php:424-425 (`feedback:prune-old-submissions` comment); config/partna.php:2666
    - **Affects:** Developer/ops understanding of how long `core.feedback` submissions (which carry `reply_email` and free-text message bodies) are retained; no runtime behaviour is affected.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Update the comment at `routes/console.php:425` from "default 365d" to "default 90d" to match `config('partna.feedback.retention_days')`.
    - **Technical:** `config/partna.php:2666` sets `feedback.retention_days` to `env('FEEDBACK_RETENTION_DAYS', 90)`. `PruneOldFeedbackSubmissionsCommand::handle()` reads `config('partna.feedback.retention_days', 90)` with no `--days` override passed by the scheduler, so the command genuinely runs at 90 days. The `routes/console.php` comment above the schedule entry still says "default 365d" — stale documentation, not a functional bug, but exactly the kind of drift that misleads an operator or a privacy reviewer trying to state the platform's actual retention posture during an incident or audit.
    - **Plain English:** One place in our rulebook says feedback messages are kept for 90 days; a sticky note next to the cleanup schedule says 365 days. The system actually follows the 90-day rule — the sticky note is just wrong — but a wrong note like this could easily mislead someone during a privacy review.

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W1-EDGE-1** · P1 — Bulk platform takedowns can delay cache purge up to 1 hour, leaving stale content visible
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob.php:113 (dispatch), config/partna.php:2793–2799 (queue config)
    - **Affects:** Compliance/safety — when a platform is globally disabled, affected sites' cached content remains publicly visible for up to 1 hour while purge jobs queue on the lowest-priority lane.
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Ensure bulk platform takedowns pass a `moderationCaseId` into every `CloudflareCachePurgeJob` dispatch so failures escalate to on-call (fixes EDGE-2 simultaneously).
        - Consider elevating bulk takedown purges to a medium-priority queue (not lowest), or implement an SLA checker that alerts if a bulk purge ages past 5 minutes on the queue.
        - Add a test that verifies `ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob` passes `moderationCaseId` on every dispatch and that `EdgePurgeEscalator` fires on purge failure.
    - **Technical:** `ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob` disables platform integrations globally or per-segment by setting `is_active = false`, then dispatches `CloudflareCachePurgeJob` with `bulk: true` but no `moderationCaseId`. The `bulk: true` flag routes the job to the `cloudflare_bulk` queue, which is lowest-priority by design (config line 2096–2101, balance=>false, strict priority). The queue can serve bulk jobs only when all higher-priority lanes are empty. The `bulk_purge_max_delay_seconds` config (line 2799, default 3600s) bounds initial dispatch offset but not queue processing time — a bulk job can still wait indefinitely once queued. Even with the 1-hour dispatch cap, a large takedown fan-out (hundreds of purges for thousands of sites) could saturate the bulk lane, delaying individual purges. Since `moderationCaseId` is null, job failures silently log without escalating — a failed takedown purge leaves stale content undetected.
    - **Plain English:** When a payment processor or booking platform gets disabled platform-wide, the backend marks all connections as inactive and queues cache purges for every affected site. But those purge jobs go to the back of a very long queue (lowest priority, behind all user edits) to avoid slowing down when a user clicks "edit." That means a site could keep showing that disabled platform in its cached copy for up to an hour, and if something goes wrong with the purge, nobody gets paged — it just silently fails. For a compliance takedown, that's a real gap.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob::handle(), line 113
        $pending = CloudflareCachePurgeJob::dispatch($subdomain, bulk: true);
        // No moderationCaseId passed
        ```
        ```php
        // CloudflareCachePurgeJob::failed(), line 281-282
        if ($this->moderationCaseId !== null) {
            app(EdgePurgeEscalator::class)->escalate($this->handle, $this->moderationCaseId);
        }
        // Silent exit if moderationCaseId is null
        ```
        ```php
        // config/partna.php, line 2793-2799
        'bulk_purge_stagger_seconds' => (int) env('PARTNA_CACHE_BULK_PURGE_STAGGER_SECONDS', 0),
        'bulk_purge_max_delay_seconds' => (int) env('PARTNA_CACHE_BULK_PURGE_MAX_DELAY_SECONDS', 3600),
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): PARTIALLY STALE; the real defect underneath it is fixed and the escalation half is WONTFIX. (a) "up to 1 hour" is false by default — bulk_purge_stagger_seconds defaults to 0 (config/partna.php:2802) and ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob.php:115 dispatches at T+0 unless an ops opt-in sets it; the 3600s cap bounds a delay that is disabled. (b) "lowest-priority lane" is true but deliberate and documented (config/horizon.php:256, job docblock :33-35). (c) "nobody gets paged" is half false — CloudflareCachePurgeJob.php:275 and ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob.php:142 both report() on terminal failure. (d) The prescribed moderationCaseId is UNIMPLEMENTABLE: the only callers are StaffFeatureAvailabilityController:63 and StaffSegmentController:151, a feature-availability rule and a segment rule, neither of which has a moderation case — passing one would hand on-call a dangling reference. Escalation half closed WONTFIX. THE REAL DEFECT, which the finding missed: the takedown fired cache lane 3 ONLY, never advancing site.sites.updated_at, so the edge purge was partly self-defeating — it evicted the CDN and the next visitor refilled it from a still-stale origin cache. Fixed by adding lane 2 (one batched site.sites updated_at UPDATE per chunk, plus raiseResolveFloor per subdomain so the 30s handle.resolve cache stops serving the pre-takedown timestamp). Deliberately NOT routed through SiteCacheLanes::bust(), which delays lane 3 by 15s — wrong for a compliance takedown.

**#EDGE-2 (draft: Bulk takedown purge failures may not page on-call)** — MERGED INTO EDGE-1. The root cause is that `ReconcilePlatformTakedownJob` does not pass `moderationCaseId`. Fixing EDGE-1 (pass the case ID on dispatch) automatically fixes the escalation gap.

**#EDGE-3 (draft: Backend config lacks mirror-sync comments)** — NOT A FINDING. The sync point already exists and is enforced. The Worker's `RESERVED` set (index.js:63–67) carries a documented `@sync` comment pointing to `tests/Feature/Subdomain/ReservedSubdomainWorkerSyncTest.php`, which verifies the two lists match on every test run. Drift would be caught by CI and is not a drift risk.
