## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-1** · P2 — Unjittered int TTLs on Google Business Apify idempotency markers
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Platforms/GoogleBusinessEnrichJob.php:193, :196 (TTL constants at :71, :73)
    - **Affects:** Paid Apify enrichment in-flight/result markers in Redis; fleet-wide synchronized expiry can trigger a re-bill/re-fetch burst at the 900s/1800s expiry boundary.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Route both `Cache::put` writes through `JitteredTtl::applyJitter($ttl)` before passing the TTL, or via `CacheLockService`.
        - Keep the TTL constants (`APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL` = 900, `APIFY_RESULT_TTL` = 1800) as the base; apply jitter at the write site.
    - **Technical:** Category 2 (unjittered TTLs). `Cache::put($inflightKey, ..., self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL)` and `Cache::put($resultKey, ..., self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL)` pass hardcoded int TTLs directly, bypassing `JitteredTtl::applyJitter`. Both keys are already correctly sourced from `CacheKeyGenerator::googleBusinessApifyInflight/Result` (no key-drift issue), so this is purely a jitter gap. Every worker enrichment for the same place synchronizes on the same 900s/1800s clock, which is the exact thundering-herd condition jitter exists to prevent — worse here because the underlying resource is a paid Apify call.
    - **Plain English:** Two timers control how long we remember a paid Google-listing data pull before allowing another one. Both are set to the exact same length everywhere, so when they expire, every job hitting that boundary can pile up a fresh paid request at once. A small random stagger spreads that out.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($inflightKey, now()->toIso8601String(), self::APIFY_INFLIGHT_TTL);
        // ...
        Cache::put($resultKey, ['enrichment' => $enrichment], self::APIFY_RESULT_TTL);
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-2** · P2 — Unjittered `DateTimeInterface` TTL on bio-mention profile cache
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/PreAccount/BioMentionChainsJob.php:205 (`mentionProfile`)
    - **Affects:** Cache-expiry distribution across queue workers for the globally-shared `bio_mention_profile:<handle>` cache; thundering-herd risk when many pre-account builds mention the same Instagram handle.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Fold this write into the `CacheLockService::rememberLocked` replacement (see #CCH-4), which applies `JitteredTtl::applyJitter` automatically.
        - If kept standalone, convert the 14-day `DateTimeInterface` TTL to an int-second TTL and call `JitteredTtl::applyJitter($ttl)` explicitly — `DateTimeInterface` TTLs sidestep the jitter helper entirely.
    - **Technical:** Category 2. `Cache::put($key, $essentials, now()->addDays(self::CACHE_TTL_DAYS))` writes an absolute `Carbon` TTL. Every job that caches the same mentioned handle during a scrape wave stamps the identical absolute expiry, so all copies expire in lockstep and can trigger a synchronized re-scrape burst against Instagram — the docblock's own stated goal ("scraped once, not per signup") is undermined at the TTL boundary.
    - **Plain English:** Every cached copy of a mentioned Instagram profile is stamped with the exact same expiry date. When that date hits, every job that has it cached rushes to fetch a fresh copy at once. Randomising each copy's expiry a little spreads that rush out.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        Cache::put($key, $essentials, now()->addDays(self::CACHE_TTL_DAYS));
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-3** · P2 — Bio-mention profile cache has no stale-while-revalidate companion
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/PreAccount/BioMentionChainsJob.php:187–207 (`mentionProfile`)
    - **Affects:** Job latency and upstream Instagram scrape pressure once the cached mention profile expires; no last-good value is served while a fresh fetch runs.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h), folds into #CCH-4's fix
    - **What to do:**
        - Replace with `CacheLockService::rememberLocked`, which supplies the `:stale` companion for this type of positive cached value.
    - **Technical:** Category 3. After TTL expiry, `Cache::get($key)` misses and the job synchronously pays for a fresh scrape with no `$key:stale` last-good fallback — every concurrent caller for that handle must wait on (or independently trigger) recomputation rather than receiving the last-good profile while a single lock-holder refreshes silently.
    - **Plain English:** When the cached profile expires, it's gone entirely, and whoever asks next has to wait for — and pay for — a fresh fetch. A better setup would keep serving the old copy while quietly refreshing it in the background, so nobody has to wait.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $cached = Cache::get($key);
        if (is_array($cached)) {
            return $cached;
        }

        $scrapes++;
        $result = $scraper->fetchProfileResult($handle, $this->userId);
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-4** · P2 — Bio-mention profile cache has no single-flight lock for a globally-shared paid scrape
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/PreAccount/BioMentionChainsJob.php:185–208 (`mentionProfile`)
    - **Affects:** Pre-account Instagram bio-mention chaining; Apify/InstagramScraper cost and throughput when multiple users' builds mention the same handle within the 14-day cache window.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Inject `CacheLockService` and replace the `Cache::get` → recompute → `Cache::put` sequence with `$this->cache->rememberLocked($key, $ttl, fn() => ...)`.
        - Use a `CacheKeyGenerator`-derived key (see #CCH-6) and a bounded, jittered TTL so exactly one job regenerates the profile per handle while concurrent jobs block on the lock (or read stale — see #CCH-3).
    - **Technical:** Category 1. `BioMentionChainsJob::uniqueId()` returns `$this->userId`, so `ShouldBeUnique` only dedupes per-user, not per mentioned handle. Because the same brand/venue handle appears in many different users' pre-account bios (the docblock's own example: `@andisco_aunz` "appears in hundreds of barber bios"), concurrent jobs for *different* users mentioning the same handle each see a `Cache::get` miss before any `Cache::put` lands, and each independently pays for `InstagramScraper::fetchProfileResult`. This directly defeats the stated cost-control intent ("scraped once, not per signup"). `CacheLockService::rememberLocked` closes the gap with a single-flight Redis lock.
    - **Plain English:** This is like ten different people finding the same empty file drawer and each ordering their own expensive replacement, instead of one person ordering it while the others wait for that one copy. It wastes money on repeat paid lookups and can overload the upstream service.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $key = 'bio_mention_profile:'.$handle;
        $cached = Cache::get($key);
        if (is_array($cached)) {
            return $cached;
        }

        $scrapes++;
        $result = $scraper->fetchProfileResult($handle, $this->userId);
        $profile = $result->profile;
        if ($profile === null) {
            return null;
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-5** · P2 — Public-payload action list caches an empty result on a transient DB fault without marking the build degraded
    - **Where:** app/Services/PublicSite/IndividualProfilePayloadBuilder.php:286–291 (`buildActions`)
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage visitors whose profile payload is built while `ActionCandidates::forSite()` throws a `QueryException`; they receive an empty `actions` section for the full (jittered) payload TTL instead of the short degraded TTL.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Call `$this->resolver->markDegraded()` in the `QueryException` catch, matching the pattern already used two call sites away (the `pageOrder()` popularity-rank path and the `actionRanksForSite` smart-mode branch in the same method) so the controller shortens the cache TTL via `IndividualProfileController`'s existing `shortenDegraded($key, $payload, $this->builder->degradedCacheTtl())` call.
    - **Technical:** Category 10 (read-through that hides errors) — verified against the caller: `IndividualProfileController` wraps `$this->builder->build(...)` inside `CacheLockService::rememberLocked`, then checks `$this->builder->lastBuildDegraded()` and calls `$this->cache->shortenDegraded($key, $payload, $this->builder->degradedCacheTtl())` when a build ran degraded — this is exactly the seam `buildActions()` should use but doesn't. The same method already calls `markDegraded()` on the `smart`-mode `actionRanksForSite` failure path a few lines below (line 298) and `pageOrder()` calls it on `pageRanksFromActions` failure (line 231) — the `QueryException` catch around `ActionCandidates::forSite()` is the one path in this class that swallows a DB fault into a plain empty-array without flagging degradation, so the resulting empty `actions` list rides the full normal-mode TTL (jittered ~60s) instead of the ~10s degraded TTL.
    - **Plain English:** If the database stumbles for a moment while a public page is being built, the "highlighted actions" section on that page quietly comes back empty — and that empty version gets remembered and shown to every visitor for about a minute, instead of just a few seconds. The code already has a "mark this build as shaky, don't remember it for long" switch, and uses it in two nearby places for similar faults — it's just missing here.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        try {
            $candidates = $this->candidates->forSite($pro, $site, $sections, $pools);
        } catch (QueryException $e) {
            Log::warning('sitepage.actions_candidates_failed', ['site_id' => $site->id, 'error' => $e->getMessage()]);
            $candidates = [];
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W2-CCH-6** · P3 — Bio-mention profile cache key built by string concatenation instead of `CacheKeyGenerator`
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/PreAccount/BioMentionChainsJob.php:187 (`mentionProfile`)
    - **Affects:** Future readers/invalidators of this cache entry; key drift risk if another call site (e.g. a future admin tool or metrics job) constructs the same logical key differently.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add a helper, e.g. `CacheKeyGenerator::bioMentionProfile(string $handle): string`, and use it here (folding naturally into the #CCH-4 rewrite).
    - **Technical:** Category 8. The key is built via `'bio_mention_profile:'.$handle` rather than a centralised generator. Risk is currently bounded — both the read and the write sit in the same method — but the gold standard requires every key for a given cached value to originate from one helper so reader/writer keys can't drift as new call sites are added.
    - **Plain English:** The label on this cached record is handwritten right where it's used. If someone later writes a slightly different version of that label elsewhere, they won't find the existing record. A standard label-maker keeps every copy findable under one name.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $key = 'bio_mention_profile:'.$handle;
        $cached = Cache::get($key);
        ```

## Suggested Bundled Sessions

- **Bundle 1 — BioMentionChainsJob cache hygiene:** #CCH-2, #CCH-3, #CCH-4, #CCH-6
    - **Why grouped:** all four are deviations at the same call site (`mentionProfile()`'s manual `Cache::get`/`Cache::put` pair); the single canonical fix — replace with `CacheLockService::rememberLocked($key, applyJitter'd TTL, closure)` using a new `CacheKeyGenerator::bioMentionProfile()` key — resolves all four at once.
    - **Model:** follow the file's Execution policy (Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet).

- **Bundle 2 — Google Business Apify TTL jitter:** #CCH-1

## Standalone — do NOT bundle

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W2-DINT-1** · P1 — `content.f_review.staff_name` (the professional's own name) is never selected into the GDPR data-export payload
    - **Where:** app/Services/User/DataExport/DataExportPayloadBuilder.php:576, app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:64
    - **Affects:** Any professional whose Fresha reviews carry structured staff attribution (`staff_name`) and who requests a data export (Art. 15/DSAR).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `content.f_review.staff_name` to the `select()` list in `DataExportPayloadBuilder::streamContentFReview()` (currently `item_id, source_id, rating, reviewed_at, updated_at` only).
        - Do NOT add `author_name`/`author_photo_url`/`text` to the same select — those remain deliberately excluded per the `#PRIV-3` doctrine in `AccountDeletionService` (third-party reviewer PII, out of scope for the account holder's own export).
        - Add a regression assertion that an export for a user with a Fresha-sourced review containing `staff_name` includes that value.
    - **Technical:** Migration `20260828030000_f_review_staff_name.sql` added the column specifically because `FreshaReviewProjector` had been emitting `staff_name` since T23b but `ProjectionWriter`'s `SINGLETON_FACETS['f_review']` column list silently dropped it at write time; the migration's own comment states "It is the professional's OWN name (never reviewer PII)." Deletion is already correctly handled — `AccountDeletionService::PURGED_PII_TABLES`'s docblock explains the whole `content.*`/`ingest.*` tree (including `content.f_review`) is reached by an FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `core.users` through `content.sources`/`content.items`, so no explicit purge call is needed there. But `DataExportPayloadBuilder::streamContentFReview()` was written before `staff_name` existed and was never updated, so a professional's own data-export request omits a piece of their own identity that the platform stores about them.
    - **Plain English:** When Fresha tells us which staff member a review is about, we now save that person's name alongside the review. If that person asks for a copy of everything we hold about them, this particular name is quietly left out of the download — like handing someone their file folder with one page missing, even though the page is filed correctly and gets shredded correctly when they close their account. It just never gets copied when they ask to see it.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        'f_review' => ['author_name', 'author_photo_url', 'author_uri', 'rating', 'text', 'reviewed_at', 'staff_name'],
        ```
        ```php
        ->select(['content.f_review.item_id', 'content.f_review.source_id', 'content.f_review.rating', 'content.f_review.reviewed_at', 'content.f_review.updated_at'])
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): FIXED — same bug as #W1-PRIV-2, same fix, one change. See that finding for the full reasoning, including why the prescribed unconditional select was rejected (staff_name can name a co-worker on a storewide venue source, not only the requester).

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W2-DINT-2** · P2 — `ManualMediaWriter::remove()` swallows every `\Throwable` from the content-anchor lookup, not just the documented "content lane absent" case
    - **Where:** app/Services/Content/ManualMediaWriter.php:99-111
    - **Affects:** Dashboard media deletion; risk of a `content.items` row left pointing at a `site.site_media` row that the caller then deletes.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Narrow the catch to the documented case: a bare `content.item_anchors` table-missing error (SQLite test stand-in / a partial env), e.g. by catching `\Illuminate\Database\QueryException` and checking the SQLSTATE (`42P01`) rather than `\Throwable`.
        - Re-throw (or `report()` + rethrow) any other database exception so the caller does not proceed to delete the `SiteMedia` bytes while a live `content.items` row still references them.
        - Follow the pattern already established in `IntegrationConnectionObserver::syncIngestSource()` in the same codebase, which distinguishes `UniqueConstraintViolationException` (quiet), `QueryException` (`report()` + `Log::warning`) and `\Throwable` (`report()` + `Log::warning`) rather than swallowing everything silently.
    - **Technical:** The catch comment justifies fail-open only for "the content lane itself is absent (partial test envs)" — true in production too, since `content.*` doesn't exist there and every call would throw `42P01` there by design. But in dev/staging, where the schema exists, a transient failure (connection blip, statement timeout, permission error) during this specific lookup is caught by the same blanket `catch (\Throwable)` and silently returns, after which the caller is free to hard-delete the `SiteMedia` row/bytes while `content.items.media[*].site_media_id` (a JSONB pointer with no DB-level FK — Postgres cannot enforce a foreign key into JSONB) still references it. This is a narrow, low-frequency window, not a routine failure mode.
    - **Plain English:** Before deleting an uploaded photo, the system is supposed to first check "is this photo shown anywhere on the site?" and mark that spot as empty. If that check itself briefly fails for an unrelated reason, the code currently shrugs and carries on as if the check succeeded — then the photo gets deleted anyway, leaving a display slot pointing at a photo that no longer exists. It should stop and report the failure instead of assuming the best case.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        try {
            $itemId = DB::connection('pgsql')->table('content.item_anchors')
                ->where('user_id', $userId)
                ->where('coord', self::coordFor($media))
                ->value('item_id');
        } catch (\Throwable) {
            // Fail-open ONLY here: a lookup that throws means the content
            // lane itself is absent (partial test envs), so no bridged item
            // exists to strand. ...
            return;
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W2-DINT-3** · P3 — `ProjectionWriter::ensureContentSource()` does a non-atomic SELECT-then-INSERT, unlike its sibling `ensureManualSource()`
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:379-399
    - **Affects:** Concurrent connection-source provisioning for the same `connection_id` (e.g. an overlapping scheduled run and a manual resync).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Change the insert to `insertOrIgnore()` followed by a re-read keyed on `connection_id`, mirroring `ensureManualSource()`'s existing pattern one function below it.
    - **Technical:** `content.sources` already carries `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "idx_content_sources_connection" ON "content"."sources" ("connection_id") WHERE ("connection_id" IS NOT NULL)` (`20260727140000_content_schema.sql`), so a concurrent double-insert cannot actually produce duplicate rows — this is narrower than DeepSeek's original "content.sources can become duplicated" claim. The real gap: the second of two racing callers gets an uncaught `23505` `QueryException` instead of gracefully returning the winner's id, unlike `ensureManualSource()` immediately below it in the same file, which already uses `insertOrIgnore()` + re-read specifically to avoid this.
    - **Plain English:** Two processes creating a record for the same connected platform account at the same instant can both check "does this exist yet?", both get "no", and then race to create it. The database's own safety net stops a duplicate from actually being saved, but the process that loses the race crashes with an error instead of just quietly picking up the record the winner made — the same problem this file's own neighboring function already solved.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $existing = DB::table('content.sources')->where('connection_id', $connectionId)->value('id');
        if ($existing !== null) {
            return (string) $existing;
        }

        $id = (string) Str::uuid();
        DB::table('content.sources')->insert([
            'id' => $id,
            'user_id' => $userId,
            'kind' => 'connection',
            'connection_id' => $connectionId,
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-DINT-4** · P3 — `site.site_media.pool` DB CHECK allows `'product'`, which has no `SiteMedia` app constant or handling
    - **Where:** app/Models/Core/Site/SiteMedia.php:57-67, supabase/migrations/20260726000000_baseline_pilot.sql:1977
    - **Affects:** Media-pool filters/queries; the DB can hold or accept a `pool` value the app has no named constant for.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Either add `POOL_PRODUCT` and real handling if the value is meant to be reachable, or narrow `site_media_pool_check` to the four values the app actually models (`gallery`, `content`, `documents`, `design`) if `'product'` is dead vocabulary from a removed feature.
    - **Technical:** The live constraint (verified against the current migration set — no later migration alters it) is `CONSTRAINT "site_media_pool_check" CHECK (("pool")::"text" = ANY (ARRAY['gallery','content','design','product','documents']))`. `SiteMedia` defines `POOL_GALLERY`, `POOL_CONTENT`, `POOL_DESIGN`, `POOL_DOCUMENTS` but no `POOL_PRODUCT`, and no controller in `app/Http/Controllers/Api/User/` writes `pool => 'product'`. This is drift in the other direction from the lens's canonical example (`'brand_gallery'` is not in the current CHECK at all — it appears to have already been cleaned up) but the same pattern: the DB accepts a value the app's own vocabulary doesn't name.
    - **Plain English:** The database's rulebook for "what kind of media file is this" has an entry ("product") that the application's own rulebook doesn't define anywhere. Nothing is broken today because nothing writes that value, but the two rulebooks have quietly drifted apart.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```sql
        CONSTRAINT "site_media_pool_check" CHECK ((("pool")::"text" = ANY ((ARRAY['gallery'::character varying, 'content'::character varying, 'design'::character varying, 'product'::character varying, 'documents'::character varying])::"text"[]))),
        ```
        ```php
        public const POOL_GALLERY = 'gallery';
        public const POOL_CONTENT = 'content';
        public const POOL_DOCUMENTS = 'documents';
        public const POOL_DESIGN = 'design';
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-DINT-5** · P3 — `site.site_media.scanned_at` is not cast to Carbon, unlike every other timestamp column on the model
    - **Where:** app/Models/Core/Site/SiteMedia.php:35, 177-185
    - **Affects:** Any future code reading `SiteMedia::scanned_at` expecting Carbon semantics (timezone conversion, `diffForHumans()`, comparisons).
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `'scanned_at' => 'datetime'` to `$casts`.
        - Grep for any existing `scanned_at` consumer and confirm it isn't relying on the raw-string quirk before changing it.
    - **Technical:** The class's own docblock flags this explicitly: `scanned_at` "is NOT in $casts, so unlike every other timestamp column here this returns a raw driver string, not a Carbon instance." `$casts` lists `created_at`/`updated_at` as `'datetime'` but omits `scanned_at`, confirmed by direct read of the property array.
    - **Plain English:** Almost every date field on this record comes out ready to format and compare. One date field — when a photo finished its safety scan — comes out as plain text instead. It isn't causing a problem today, but it's an inconsistency waiting to trip up whoever writes the next piece of code that touches it.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        protected $casts = [
            'sort_order' => 'integer',
            'is_active' => 'boolean',
            'original_size_bytes' => 'integer',
            'duration_ms' => 'integer',
            'palette' => 'array',
            'created_at' => 'datetime',
            'updated_at' => 'datetime',
        ];
        ```

## P2 — Should fix

- [x] **#W2-JOB-1** · P2 — `SuspendSiteJob` has neither a completed-status short-circuit nor `ShouldBeUnique`
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/SuspendSiteJob.php:16, 42-61
    - **Affects:** `ActionLogEntry` audit-trail integrity for site suspensions; wasted redelivery work
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Mirror `NotifyOnCallStaffJob`'s pattern exactly: add `if ($entry->status === 'completed') { return; }` immediately after loading the entry, before `markDispatched()`.
        - Implement `ShouldBeUnique` with `uniqueId(): string { return $this->actionLogId; }` and a `$uniqueFor` matching the job's `$timeout` (60) plus headroom, same as `NotifyOnCallStaffJob`'s `uniqueFor = 300`.
    - **Technical:** `SuspendSiteJob::handle()` loads the `ActionLogEntry`, unconditionally calls `markDispatched()` (which increments `attempts` and overwrites `dispatched_at`), performs the site update, then `markCompleted()`. There is no check of the entry's current status before acting, and the class implements only `ShouldQueue`, not `ShouldBeUnique`. Two failure modes converge on the same gap: (1) at-least-once Redis queue delivery — a worker killed after the transaction commits but before the job is acked can be redelivered, re-running the whole handler; (2) a duplicate dispatch of the same `actionLogId` (double-click, retry race) runs concurrently with no lock. The site update itself is idempotent (`moderation_state = 'hidden'` twice is the same state), but `attempts` increments again and `dispatched_at`/`completed_at` are overwritten, polluting the moderation audit trail that `ActionLogEntry` exists to keep accurate. `NotifyOnCallStaffJob` (same file family, same `HasActionLogLifecycle` trait) already demonstrates both fixes are the intended pattern for this job family — `SuspendSiteJob`, `SuspendUserJob`, `QuarantineMediaJob` and `PurgeModerationCacheJob` are the four outliers that never received it.
    - **Plain English:** When a moderator's system decides to hide a website for breaking the rules, it should first check "have I already done this?" before doing it again. Right now it doesn't check — so if the system hiccups and re-runs the same instruction, the site still ends up correctly hidden, but the paperwork trail (who did what, when, how many tries) gets muddied, which matters when someone reviews a moderation decision later.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public function handle(): void
        {
            DB::connection('pgsql')->transaction(function () {
                $case = ModerationCase::query()->findOrFail($this->caseId);
                $entry = ActionLogEntry::query()->findOrFail($this->actionLogId);

                // Mark as dispatched and increment the attempt counter before acting —
                // if the site update throws, the action log reflects the attempt.
                $this->markDispatched($entry);

                $siteId = $this->resolveSiteId($case);
                if ($siteId !== null) {
                    Site::query()
                        ->where('id', $siteId)
                        ->update(['moderation_state' => 'hidden']);
                }

                $this->markCompleted($entry);
            });
        }
        ```

    - **Resolution (2026-08-30):** Fixed by `4c549e39b` + `9a5c0d621` — a completed-status guard (`if ($entry->status === 'completed') { return; }`) added at the top of `handle()`, re-reading `$entry->status` from fresh DB state inside the transaction, closes the redelivery gap this finding describes. `ShouldBeUnique` was also added but is NOT what closes this: `Bus::chain(...)->dispatch()` (`ModerationActionDispatcher::dispatchFor()`) never constructs a `PendingDispatch`, so `UniqueLock::acquire()` — which lives only in `PendingDispatch::shouldDispatch()`, run from its `__destruct()` — is never invoked for the first link of a chain, and even where it is invoked for a later chain link, a Horizon at-least-once redelivery re-enters `CallQueuedHandler::call()` directly and never touches the lock regardless. The completed-status guard is the entire protection here; do not read the lock as load-bearing.

- [x] **#W2-JOB-2** · P2 — `SuspendUserJob` has neither a completed-status short-circuit nor `ShouldBeUnique`
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/SuspendUserJob.php:16, 40-65
    - **Affects:** `ActionLogEntry` audit-trail integrity for user suspensions/bans; wasted redelivery work
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Same fix as JOB-1: `if ($entry->status === 'completed') { return; }` at the top of `handle()`, plus `ShouldBeUnique` with `uniqueId() = $this->actionLogId`.
    - **Technical:** Identical structural gap to `SuspendSiteJob` — same `HasActionLogLifecycle` trait, same unconditional `markDispatched()` before any status check, same missing `ShouldBeUnique`. The `User::status` update is idempotent (`'suspended'`/`'disabled'` written twice is the same state), so the risk is confined to attempt-counter/audit-trail pollution and a redundant concurrent write, not a functional suspension failure. Same root cause as JOB-1, JOB-3, JOB-4 — tiered identically.
    - **Plain English:** Same issue as the site-suspension job, but for banning/suspending a person's account: the system doesn't check whether it already sent this warning before sending it again, so a rare system hiccup can leave a messy paper trail even though the person ends up correctly suspended either way.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public function handle(): void
        {
            DB::connection('pgsql')->transaction(function () {
                $case = ModerationCase::query()->findOrFail($this->caseId);
                $entry = ActionLogEntry::query()->findOrFail($this->actionLogId);

                // Mark as dispatched and increment the attempt counter before acting —
                // if the user update throws, the action log reflects the attempt.
                $this->markDispatched($entry);

                if ($case->reportable_owner_user_id !== null) {
                    $decision = $case->decisions()->latest('decided_at')->first();
                    $newStatus = match ($decision?->decision_type) {
                        'ban_user' => 'disabled',
                        default => 'suspended',
                    };

                    User::query()
                        ->where('id', $case->reportable_owner_user_id)
                        ->update(['status' => $newStatus]);
                }

                $this->markCompleted($entry);
            });
        }
        ```

    - **Resolution (2026-08-30):** Fixed by `4c549e39b` + `9a5c0d621` — a completed-status guard (`if ($entry->status === 'completed') { return; }`) added at the top of `handle()`, re-reading `$entry->status` from fresh DB state inside the transaction, closes the redelivery gap this finding describes. `ShouldBeUnique` was also added but is NOT what closes this: `Bus::chain(...)->dispatch()` (`ModerationActionDispatcher::dispatchFor()`) never constructs a `PendingDispatch`, so `UniqueLock::acquire()` — which lives only in `PendingDispatch::shouldDispatch()`, run from its `__destruct()` — is never invoked for the first link of a chain, and even where it is invoked for a later chain link, a Horizon at-least-once redelivery re-enters `CallQueuedHandler::call()` directly and never touches the lock regardless. The completed-status guard is the entire protection here; do not read the lock as load-bearing.

- [x] **#W2-JOB-3** · P2 — `QuarantineMediaJob` has neither a completed-status short-circuit nor `ShouldBeUnique`
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/QuarantineMediaJob.php:19, 35-53
    - **Affects:** `ActionLogEntry` audit-trail integrity for CSAM/media quarantine actions; wasted redelivery work
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Same fix as JOB-1/JOB-2: completed-status short-circuit + `ShouldBeUnique` keyed on `actionLogId`.
    - **Technical:** Same structural gap, same trait, same missing guard. The raw `DB::update("UPDATE site.site_media SET processing_state = 'quarantined' WHERE id = ?", ...)` is idempotent by value, so redelivery does not un-quarantine or re-quarantine incorrectly — but this is the CSAM auto-action pipeline, where the accuracy of the action log (exactly one dispatch, one completion, per case) carries the most legal/compliance weight of the four jobs in this family. Same root cause as JOB-1/2/4 — tiered identically.
    - **Plain English:** This job flags a piece of uploaded media (e.g. suspected illegal content) as quarantined. It doesn't check first whether that flag was already set, so a rare system hiccup can make the compliance log look like the action ran more than once, even though the media itself only ends up in one, correct state.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public function handle(): void
        {
            DB::connection('pgsql')->transaction(function () {
                $case = ModerationCase::query()->findOrFail($this->caseId);
                $entry = ActionLogEntry::query()->findOrFail($this->actionLogId);

                $this->markDispatched($entry);

                // Prefer explicit site_media_id from action_target; fall back to reportable_id
                $mediaId = $entry->action_target['site_media_id'] ?? $case->reportable_id;

                DB::update(
                    "UPDATE site.site_media SET processing_state = 'quarantined' WHERE id = ?",
                    [$mediaId]
                );

                $this->markCompleted($entry);
            });
        }
        ```

    - **Resolution (2026-08-30):** Fixed by `4c549e39b` + `9a5c0d621` — a completed-status guard (`if ($entry->status === 'completed') { return; }`) added at the top of `handle()`, re-reading `$entry->status` from fresh DB state inside the transaction, closes the redelivery gap this finding describes. `ShouldBeUnique` was also added but is NOT what closes this: `Bus::chain(...)->dispatch()` (`ModerationActionDispatcher::dispatchFor()`) never constructs a `PendingDispatch`, so `UniqueLock::acquire()` — which lives only in `PendingDispatch::shouldDispatch()`, run from its `__destruct()` — is never invoked for the first link of a chain, and even where it is invoked for a later chain link, a Horizon at-least-once redelivery re-enters `CallQueuedHandler::call()` directly and never touches the lock regardless. The completed-status guard is the entire protection here; do not read the lock as load-bearing.

- [ ] **#W2-JOB-4** · P2 — `PurgeModerationCacheJob` can re-dispatch `SyncSubdomainToKvJob`/`CloudflareCachePurgeJob` on redelivery; no completed-status guard or `ShouldBeUnique`
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/PurgeModerationCacheJob.php:21, 38-75
    - **Affects:** Edge-cache purge and KV routing dispatch for moderation takedowns; `ActionLogEntry` audit-trail integrity
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Same completed-status short-circuit + `ShouldBeUnique` (`uniqueId() = actionLogId`) as the other three moderation jobs.
    - **Technical:** Same family, same gap — but this job's redelivery cost is slightly higher than the other three: on redelivery it re-dispatches `SyncSubdomainToKvJob::dispatch($ownerId)` and `CloudflareCachePurgeJob::dispatch(...)` unconditionally. Both downstream jobs are themselves safe against the duplicate (`CloudflareCachePurgeJob` is `ShouldBeUnique` with a 35s coalesce window per `app/Jobs/Cloudflare/CloudflareCachePurgeJob.php:113`, and `SyncSubdomainToKvJob` reconciles KV to the current DB state on every run), so the practical outcome is wasted work, not incorrect routing state. Adding the guard here closes the gap at the source rather than relying on each downstream job's own defenses. Same root cause as JOB-1/2/3 — tiered identically.
    - **Plain English:** After a moderation decision, this job tells two other systems to update a taken-down page's public routing and clear its cached copy. It doesn't check if it already gave those instructions, so a rare hiccup could send the same instructions twice — wasteful, but the other two systems are built to shrug off a duplicate, so nothing breaks for a visitor.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        public function handle(): void
        {
            $case = ModerationCase::query()->findOrFail($this->caseId);
            $entry = ActionLogEntry::query()->findOrFail($this->actionLogId);

            $this->markDispatched($entry);

            if ($case->reportable_owner_user_id !== null) {
                $ownerId = $case->reportable_owner_user_id;

                SyncSubdomainToKvJob::dispatch($ownerId);
                ...
                CloudflareCachePurgeJob::dispatch((string) $owner->handle, $customDomain, moderationCaseId: $this->caseId);
            }

            $this->markCompleted($entry);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-JOB-5** · P2 — `FleetRebuildCommand` ignores the exit code of `builds:prune-expired` before rebuilding
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/FleetRebuildCommand.php:93
    - **Affects:** Staff-run fleet rebuild tool; unclaimed-account handle allocation
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Capture `Artisan::call('builds:prune-expired', [], $this->output)`'s return value; if it is not `0` (`Command::SUCCESS`), print an error and `return self::FAILURE` before the rebuild loop runs.
    - **Technical:** `Artisan::call()` does not throw on a failing sub-command — it returns an exit code that this command discards. The rebuild sequence depends on the prune step having removed the just-expired build rows before `requestBuild()` runs, since `PreAccountBuildService::requestBuild`'s live-source dedupe would otherwise block a plain re-request. This is a manually staff-invoked CLI tool (per its own docblock: "Staff/dev use via `cloud command:run`"), and `$this->output` is passed through to `Artisan::call`, so a failure does stream visibly to the operator watching the terminal — this materially reduces the real-world risk versus an unattended path, which is why this sits at P2 (hardening) rather than P1: the failure is visible today, just not defended against programmatically.
    - **Plain English:** This staff tool cleans up old data before rebuilding a handful of accounts, but it never checks whether the cleanup actually succeeded — it just barrels ahead either way. A staff member watching the screen would likely notice a cleanup error scroll by, but the tool itself should stop and say "cleanup failed, aborting" instead of relying on someone reading the log in real time.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // builds:prune-expired prunes EVERY expired live build, not only ours —
        // by design: anything legitimately expired should go on the same sweep.
        Artisan::call('builds:prune-expired', [], $this->output);
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-JOB-6** · P2 — `FleetRebuildCommand` has no per-spec idempotency guard; a partial-failure retry re-processes already-rebuilt handles
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/FleetRebuildCommand.php:87-119
    - **Affects:** Staff-run fleet rebuild tool; unclaimed-account handle allocation, potential duplicate handle reallocation on retry
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Before calling `requestBuild()` for each spec, check whether a `PreAccountBuild` created after the prune step already exists for that `user_id`/`source_ref`; skip specs already rebuilt in this invocation.
        - Alternatively, document (in the command's help text) that a partial failure must be re-run with only the failed handles, not the full original list — the command already prints per-handle status (`$this->info`/`$this->error`) that lets an operator construct that list.
    - **Technical:** The command expires every spec's build, prunes, then loops `requestBuild()` per spec inside a per-spec `try`/`catch` that only increments a failure counter and continues. If a later spec in the list throws, the earlier specs have already been torn down and rebuilt successfully. Re-running the command with the same full handle list re-expires and re-rebuilds those already-successful specs too, because nothing distinguishes "already rebuilt this run" from "still pending" — and `requestBuild()` can allocate a *different* handle on a rebuild (the command already anticipates and warns about this: `if ($newHandle !== $spec['handle']) { $this->warn(...) }`), so a redundant rebuild is not merely wasteful, it can reallocate a handle a second time. This is a manual admin tool (effort M reflects the idempotency-tracking work, not urgency) — operators can currently avoid the risk by re-running only the handles that failed, using the per-handle status already printed.
    - **Plain English:** This staff tool tears down and rebuilds a batch of test/demo accounts by handle name. If one account in the batch fails partway through, the ones that already succeeded aren't remembered — re-running the same command list knocks them down and rebuilds them again, and a rebuild can sometimes land on a different web address than before. The safer fix is to have the tool remember what it already finished, so a retry only touches what's left.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        foreach ($specs as $spec) {
            $spec['build']->forceFill(['expires_at' => now()->subMinute()])->save();
        }
        Artisan::call('builds:prune-expired', [], $this->output);

        $failures = 0;
        foreach ($specs as $spec) {
            try {
                $result = $builds->requestBuild(
                    $spec['account_type'], $spec['source_type'], $spec['source_ref'],
                    $spec['source_name'], null, $staff,
                );
                ...
            } catch (\Throwable $e) {
                $failures++;
                $this->error("{$spec['handle']}: rebuild failed — ".$e->getMessage());
            }
        }

        return $failures === 0 ? self::SUCCESS : self::FAILURE;
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-JOB-7** · P2 — `ComputeContentPopularityScores::writeWatermark()` failure is `Log::warning`-only, invisible to Nightwatch
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:296-307
    - **Affects:** Analytics scoring sweep's incremental-window watermark; content ranking freshness
    - **Technical:** On a `QueryException` writing the `analytics.scoring_watermarks` bookkeeping row, the catch only calls `Log::warning` and the command still returns `self::SUCCESS`. Per the observability doctrine, Nightwatch alerts on exceptions and auto-detected slow jobs/routes — not on log queries — so a persistent watermark-write failure (e.g. a schema drift or permissions issue on that one table) is silently invisible to alerting. The practical consequence is bounded (the command falls back to a fixed 60-minute lookback window per the class docblock), which is why this sits at P2 rather than P1, but the failure mode is exactly the "bare `Log::warning` is invisible" pattern the codebase's own observability doctrine calls out.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report($e);` immediately before the existing `Log::warning(...)` call, mirroring the pattern used everywhere else in this same file (e.g. `computeForSite()`'s action-scoring catch at line ~482).
    - **Plain English:** This job leaves itself a bookmark after each successful run so next time it knows where to pick up. If writing that bookmark fails, right now it only scribbles a note in a giant log file that nobody actively watches — no alert fires. The fix is one line: also tell the alerting system, the same way a doctor's office pages someone instead of just filing a note.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (QueryException $e) {
            // Never fail the sweep over its bookkeeping row — but say so.
            Log::warning('analytics.scoring_watermark_write_failed', ['error' => $e->getMessage()]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-JOB-8** · P2 — `ComputeContentPopularityScores::servedPools()` swallows `QueryException` completely silently, no log or alert
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:539-546
    - **Affects:** Content popularity scoring; per-site media-dwell and action-candidate context in the smart-ranking pipeline
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report($e); Log::warning('analytics.served_pools_failed', ['site_id' => $site->id, 'error' => $e->getMessage()]);` in the catch, matching the pattern this same file already uses in `computeForSite()`'s action-scoring try/catch and `readWatermark()`'s comment ("table absent (pre-migration env)").
    - **Technical:** `servedPools()` runs once per site on every scoring sweep. A `QueryException` here — distinct from the documented "content lane absent in partial test envs" case, since that case is a deliberate design accommodation — silently returns `[]` with zero log output and zero `report()` call, worse than JOB-7's watermark failure (which at least logs). A transient DB error on one site's pool-wire query degrades that site's item scoring (drops gallery dwell and action-candidate context) for the run with no trace anywhere that anything went wrong, and this repeats every 15 minutes for as long as the underlying fault persists.
    - **Plain English:** Before scoring a site's content, this job asks another part of the system "what's actually showing on this page right now?" If that question fails to get answered, the job just quietly continues with less information and never tells anyone — not even a log entry. A brief database hiccup for one site could keep degrading that site's rankings indefinitely with zero record of why.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        private function servedPools(Site $site): array
        {
            try {
                return $this->poolWire->forSite($site, $this->resolver);
            } catch (QueryException) {
                return [];
            }
        }
        ```

## P1 — Fix before pilot launch

- [x] **#W2-OBS-1** · P1 — On-call moderation paging job marks itself "completed" when no admin staff exist
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/NotifyOnCallStaffJob.php:56-61
    - **Affects:** CSAM auto-action and case-escalation staff paging — the sole notification path for high-priority moderation events.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Treat an empty admin-staff roster as a failure, not a completion: `$this->fail(new \RuntimeException('No on-call staff available'))` instead of `markCompleted()`.
        - `HasActionLogLifecycle::failed()` (already used by this job) will then `report()`, mark the action log `failed`, and emit a structured `Log::error` automatically — no new logging code needed.
    - **Technical:** Category 1. `handle()` queries `PartnaStaff::query()->where('role', 'admin')->get()`; when the collection is empty it calls `markCompleted($entry)` and returns without throwing. No exception reaches `HasActionLogLifecycle::failed()` (verified in `app/Jobs/Moderation/Concerns/HasActionLogLifecycle.php:62-75`, which does correctly `report()` + `Log::error` + mark `'failed'` on a *thrown* exception — this job just never throws). Horizon records a clean completion and Nightwatch never sees the incident. This is the only paging mechanism for `csam_auto_suspend`/`escalate_law_enforcement`/`escalate_esafety` decisions (`ModerationActionDispatcher::ACTIONS_BY_DECISION`), so a misconfigured or emptied admin roster is a total on-call blackout with a green dashboard.
    - **Plain English:** This is like a fire alarm that, on discovering no firefighters are on shift, quietly writes "all clear" instead of sounding louder. The fire never gets reported to anyone.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        $oncall = PartnaStaff::query()->where('role', 'admin')->get();
        if ($oncall->isEmpty()) {
            $this->markCompleted($entry);

            return;
        }
        ```

- [x] **#W2-OBS-2** · P1 — Moderation enforcement jobs ignore zero-row writes and still mark the action "completed"
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/Moderation/QuarantineMediaJob.php:46-51, app/Jobs/Moderation/SuspendSiteJob.php:52-59,68-84, app/Jobs/Moderation/SuspendUserJob.php:50-63
    - **Affects:** CSAM quarantine, site suspension, and user suspension/ban enforcement — a missing target row can leave harmful content or an active account while the audit trail says "completed."
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - `QuarantineMediaJob`: capture `DB::update()`'s return value and `$this->fail(new \RuntimeException(...))` when it is `0`.
        - `SuspendSiteJob::resolveSiteId()`: when a `SiteMedia` case's owning site can't be resolved (`$media?->site_id` is `null`), fail rather than silently skipping the hide.
        - `SuspendUserJob`: capture `User::query()->update()`'s return count and fail on `0`.
        - All three already `use HasActionLogLifecycle`, so `$this->fail($e)` gets `report()` + structured `Log::error` + `status='failed'` for free.
    - **Technical:** Category 1/7. `QuarantineMediaJob` runs a raw `DB::update()` and discards the affected-row count; if `$mediaId` doesn't match a row, zero rows change and the transaction still commits `markCompleted($entry)`. `SuspendSiteJob::resolveSiteId()` returns `null` when a `SiteMedia`-typed case's media row is missing, and `handle()` treats `$siteId === null` as a no-op that still completes. `SuspendUserJob` has the same shape: `User::query()->update()`'s return count is unchecked. None of these paths throws, so Horizon and Nightwatch both see success on an enforcement action that did nothing. Contrast this with `app/Services/Media/MediaMirror.php`'s own affected-row check (`return $wrote === 1 ? true : $this->fail(...)`, see #OBS-3) — the codebase already has the correct pattern for exactly this shape elsewhere.
    - **Plain English:** The system can stamp "done" on a takedown or quarantine request even when nothing was actually taken down. If the target record doesn't match, the harmful content stays live and no one is paged.

        ```php
        $this->markCompleted($entry);
        ```

        ```php
                ->update(['moderation_state' => 'hidden']);

        $this->markCompleted($entry);
        ```


- [x] **#W2-OBS-3** · P1 — Image mirror write ignores `Storage::put()`'s false return and marks missing bytes as mirrored
    - **Where:** app/Services/Media/MediaMirror.php:319-323
    - **Affects:** Any content asset that mirrors through the image branch while `PARTNA_MEDIA_DISK` points at a non-throwing disk alias (the file's own docblock names `public_dev`). The row is marked mirrored with `mirror_attempts` reset, and nothing retries or alerts.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Capture the return of `Storage::disk(...)->put(...)` and, when it is `false`, call the same `fail()` helper the adjacent video branch already uses: `return $this->fail($assetId, 'store_failed', $sourceUrl, 'the disk rejected the write', $userId);` (see the video branch at line 237 for the exact pattern to mirror).
        - Add a regression test with a non-throwing fake disk asserting a `false` return produces a row-level failure reason and does not clear mirror state.
    - **Technical:** Category 1. The video branch of `stream()` (lines 220-239) already guards `$stored === false` for `writeStream()`, with an explicit comment noting the media disk can be configured to a non-throwing alias where "a failed write is a false return... Unchecked, that wrote a storage_path for an object that does not exist and reported success." The image branch three lines later at 320 (`Storage::disk(config('partna.media_disk'))->put($path, $variant['bytes']);`) catches only `\Throwable` and discards `put()`'s boolean return, then proceeds to `content.media_assets`'s `storage_path`/`mime_type`/dimensions update plus `clearedMirrorState()` (which resets `mirror_attempts` to 0). This is the exact failure mode the video branch was hardened against, left open one branch over.
    - **Plain English:** The service saves media in two places. One place checks the storage system's receipt before marking the job done; the other place throws the receipt away. If storage silently fails, the image never actually gets saved, but the system clears the warning light and reports success anyway.
    - **Evidence:**

        ```php
            Storage::disk(config('partna.media_disk'))->put($path, $variant['bytes']);
        } catch (\Throwable $e) {
            return $this->fail($assetId, 'store_failed', $sourceUrl, $e->getMessage(), $userId);
        }
        ```

        ```php
            if ($stored === false) {
                return $this->fail($assetId, 'store_failed', $sourceUrl, 'the disk rejected the write', $userId);
            }
        ```

- [x] **#W2-OBS-4** · P1 — YouTube upstream fetch failures return null with only `Log::warning`, so a permanently dropped channel connection is invisible to on-call
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/YoutubeScraper.php:155-172 (fetchUploadsFeed), :283-301 (apiChannelId — see #OBS-13)
    - **Affects:** YouTube channel connect/refresh for all users; the comment at line 130 documents a *live-verified* 2026-08-21 incident (three consecutive requests answering 500/404/200) where "a terminal miss permanently drops the channel... nobody watching a modal to retry."
    - **Effort:** M (~2–4h)
    - **What to do:**
        - After the existing retry loop (lines 147-153) is exhausted, `report()` a domain exception carrying `channelId`/reason before returning `null` from `fetchUploadsFeed()`.
        - In consumers of these methods (fetch strategies / `ConnectFetchJob`), catch and `$this->fail($e)` so Horizon records a real failure and Nightwatch alerts.
        - Keep the existing `Log::warning` breadcrumbs — add reporting, don't replace them.
    - **Technical:** Category 1. Nightwatch alerts on exceptions and failed jobs, not on log queries. `fetchUploadsFeed()` already retries up to 4 times with spaced delays (a mitigation calibrated from the live 2026-08-21 incident), but once retries exhaust, both the `fetch_null` and `non_200:*` paths convert the failure to `Log::warning` + `return null` with no `report()`. The comment block explicitly documents that this results in a channel connection being permanently dropped with "nobody watching a modal to retry" — i.e. the code's own author already identified the consequence; only the alerting half is missing.
    - **Plain English:** If YouTube has a bad day, the code retries a few times, then quietly writes "couldn't fetch" in a journal nobody reads and gives up for good. The user's video feed silently goes stale — and stays that way — while the dashboard stays green.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($rss === null) {
            // LIFE-26: transport-level failure (SSRF/timeout/DNS) reaching the feed.
            Log::warning('youtube.uploads_feed_failed', ['channelId' => $channelId, 'reason' => 'fetch_null']);

            return null;
        }
        ...
        if ($rss['status'] !== 200) {
            // LIFE-26: reachable but an explicit non-200 (e.g. 403/404/5xx).
            Log::warning('youtube.uploads_feed_failed', ['channelId' => $channelId, 'reason' => 'non_200:'.$rss['status']]);

            return null;
        }
        ```
    - Resolution (2026-08-28): PARTIALLY STALE — narrowed, not implemented as prescribed. The quoted Log::warning lines are live, but the stated consequence is not: (a) ConnectFetchJob::markTerminal (T2, 2026-08-27, AFTER the wave-1 scan sha) now retries a system-initiated first-fetch miss on SYSTEM_RETRY_DELAYS = [300,900,2700,7200] before F26 deletes the row, so one transient miss no longer drops the channel; (b) the REFRESH lane already carries the failure — YoutubeFetch/YoutubeMusicFetch throw FetchUnavailableException, PlatformRefresher::recordFailure increments consecutive_failures, and PlatformHealthNotifier::connectionRefreshFailing fires a critical in-app+email notice at the breaker trip. The prescribed fix is REJECTED as written: report() inside fetchUploadsFeed() would fire on every refresh tick for every dead channel — the exact noise PlatformRefresher.php:84-88 rules out by design — and $this->fail() in ConnectFetchJob would defeat the T2 retry chain. Fixed only the two genuinely unobserved sites: the post-retry system-initiated abandonment in markTerminal (was Log::info only, row then hard-deleted, no breaker, no notifier) and YoutubeScraper::apiChannelId's 401/403 on the shared YOUTUBE_DATA_API_KEY (was a silent fallback to the scrape leg, forever). fetchUploadsFeed keeps a comment recording why it stays quiet, so this is not re-filed.

- [x] **#W2-OBS-5** · P1 — Apify account-fault statuses (401/402/403/404) on the social-actor driver are logged but never reported to Nightwatch
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Runtime/Effects/SocialActorDriver.php:100-116
    - **Affects:** TikTok and Facebook social-feed scraping for every user connecting either platform; on-call visibility into a revoked Apify token, exhausted budget, or deactivated actor.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Call `report(new \RuntimeException(...))` for `self::ACCOUNT_FAULT_STATUSES` (401/402/403/404) in addition to the existing `$status >= 500` branch — these are, by the code's own comment, "a verdict on OUR account, not on this profile," which is exactly the class of failure that needs a page.
        - Keep the `BilledEffectResult::noAnswer(...)` return (correct — it must not fail the per-profile digest run); only add the report.
    - **Technical:** Category 1/4. The driver already reports `$status >= 500` (line 105-107) but deliberately does not report 401/402/403/404 even though its own comment identifies them as account-level faults, not profile-level misses. A revoked Apify token or an exhausted paid-actor budget therefore disables all TikTok and Facebook scraping platform-wide with only an `Log::info`-level breadcrumb (see #OBS-20) and no exception ever reaching Nightwatch.
    - **Plain English:** Imagine the social-media import workers both silently shrug when their paid access card stops working, and file a note in a cabinet nobody checks. The founder sees stale social content and has no idea the whole pipeline is down, while the engineering dashboard stays green.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if (! $response->successful()) {
            $status = $response->status();
            $this->log('social.actor.not_ok', $name, $ctx, ['status' => $status]);

            if ($status >= 500) {
                report(new \RuntimeException("Apify {$name} actor '{$actor}' failed with status {$status}"));
            }

            if (in_array($status, self::ACCOUNT_FAULT_STATUSES, true)) {
                return BilledEffectResult::noAnswer(
                    "{$name} actor returned {$status} — account or actor fault, not a verdict on the profile"
                );
            }
        ```

## P2 — Should fix

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-6** · P2 — Analytics scoring watermark write failure only logs a warning, so a sustained failure is invisible to Nightwatch
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:296-307 (writeWatermark)
    - **Affects:** Popularity-scoring sweep precision — a persistent watermark write failure widens every scoped run back to a fixed 60-minute lookback with no operator page.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - In `writeWatermark()`'s catch block, add `report($e)` alongside the existing `Log::warning`, so a sustained failure (not a one-off) reaches Nightwatch.
        - Keep the "never fail the sweep over its bookkeeping row" behaviour — this command's schedule entry already has `->onFailure($reportScheduledFailure('compute-popularity'))` (`routes/console.php:144-149`), which only fires if the command itself throws or exits non-zero; a caught-and-swallowed `QueryException` here never reaches that hook.
    - **Technical:** Category 4/5. Nightwatch alerts on reported exceptions and command-level failures, not on log queries. `writeWatermark()` catches `QueryException`, logs at `warning`, and returns normally — the command still exits `SUCCESS`, so the schedule's `onFailure` hook (which does correctly `report()`) never engages for this specific fault. A persistent failure in `analytics.scoring_watermarks` (permissions, connection issue) is a real production anomaly the baseline migration guarantees the table exists for — this is not the same class of "pre-migration/test env" fallback as the read-side catches in #OBS-7.
    - **Plain English:** The popularity calculator finishes its work but can't save its "last time I ran" bookmark. It leaves itself a sticky note and reports "all good" anyway. If that bookmark keeps failing, every run scans a wider (less efficient, less precise) window — but nobody gets paged, because the alarm only rings for crashes, not sticky notes.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (QueryException $e) {
            // Never fail the sweep over its bookkeeping row — but say so.
            Log::warning('analytics.scoring_watermark_write_failed', ['error' => $e->getMessage()]);
        }
        ```

In production the `content.*` and `site.*` schemas are guaranteed present by the baseline migration, so a permission or connection failure here would be masked on every site scored, every 15 minutes, with `analytics:compute-popularity` still reporting `SUCCESS` via `routes/console.php`'s `onFailure` hook never firing.

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-8** · P2 — `$timeout` on `ComputeContentPopularityScores` is inert; a hung scoring sweep has no Nightwatch slowness signal
    - **Where:** app/Console/Commands/ComputeContentPopularityScores.php:78-82
    - **Affects:** Scheduler visibility into the popularity-scoring sweep. A hung or pathologically slow run is invisible until `routes/console.php`'s `withoutOverlapping(16)` lock forces the next tick to skip.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h) — a lightweight duration check, not a job conversion
    - **What to do:**
        - The code's own comment already documents that `Illuminate\Console\Command` never enforces this property (unlike a `ShouldQueue` job's `$timeout`). Rather than converting the command to a queued job (a large, risky change given its size and the surrounding scoring logic), wrap `handle()`'s body with a duration measurement and `report()` a warning-level exception if the run exceeds a threshold (e.g. 8 of the 16-minute lock window), so Nightwatch's slow-command detection has a real signal to key off before the lock silently absorbs the overrun.
        - Alternatively, confirm Nightwatch's command-level slow-detection already keys off wall-clock execution time (not this property) and drop this if so — the property's only problem is that it reads as protective when it is not.
    - **Technical:** Category 3. `protected $timeout = 600;` carries an accurate comment: "Illuminate\Console\Command never reads $timeout." The schedule entry (`routes/console.php:144-149`) mitigates pile-up via `withoutOverlapping(16)` and reports genuine command failures via `onFailure`, but neither of those fires on a merely-slow (not-yet-timed-out, not-failed) run — there is no explicit signal today that this specific command is running long, only that the next tick got skipped.
    - **Plain English:** The team put a speedometer on the dashboard, but the wire isn't connected. If this job slows down or stalls, the monitoring system has no early signal — it only notices once the next scheduled run gets skipped.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        // Documentation only — Illuminate\Console\Command never reads $timeout (unlike
        // the enforced, identically-named property on a ShouldQueue job).
        protected $timeout = 600;
        ```

    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report(new \RuntimeException("identity candidate cap hit for user {$userId} kind {$kind}: ".count($resolution->cappedKeys).' keys capped'))` alongside the existing bounded `Log::warning`.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. The code's own comment explicitly states "a SILENT cap on candidate pairing... is indistinguishable on a green run from 'there was nothing to suggest'" and deliberately bounds the log to one line per run — good log hygiene — but the visibility mechanism stops at `Log::warning`, which Nightwatch does not alert on. A sustained cap-hit for a large-catalogue user is therefore invisible to on-call exactly as the comment warns against, just via a different channel (log query vs. exception).
    - **Plain English:** The duplicate finder has a safety limit. When it hits that limit, it writes itself a journal entry but doesn't ring anyone. For a large account, "these two items look the same" suggestions quietly stop appearing and the dashboard still shows all green.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($resolution->cappedKeys !== []) {
            Log::warning('identity candidate cap hit — some duplicate suggestions were not recorded', [
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'kind' => $kind,
                'capped_key_count' => count($resolution->cappedKeys),
                'sample' => array_slice($resolution->cappedKeys, 0, 5, preserve_keys: true),
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-15** · P2 — Media fold cap drop is warning-only; discarded media during item merge has no Nightwatch alert
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Projection/ProjectionWriter.php:1762-1771
    - **Affects:** Users whose merged items lose incoming media rows; manual-source items in particular have no later re-projection to recover the dropped media.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report(new \RuntimeException("content.merge_fold.media_capped for user {$userId} item {$keptItemId}: dropped {$mediaDropped}"))` alongside the existing `Log::warning`.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. The code comment states plainly: "A silent cap is a defect in this codebase" — the authors already identified this as a defect, but the only implemented mitigation is a structured `Log::warning`, which Nightwatch does not alert on. This is user-visible data loss (photos dropped during a merge) with no path to operator awareness beyond someone actively reading Cloud logs.
    - **Plain English:** When the system merges two copies of the same item, it only carries over a limited number of photos and quietly drops the rest. It leaves itself a note, but no one is paged — the owner discovers missing photos before the engineering team does.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        if ($mediaDropped > 0) {
            // A silent cap is a defect in this codebase. user + item so it is
            // attributable.
            Log::warning('content.merge_fold.media_capped', [
                'user_id' => $userId,
                'item_id' => $keptItemId,
                'kept' => $mediaHeld,
                'dropped' => $mediaDropped,
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-16** · P2 — Public timestamp formatter swallows every parse failure with no breadcrumb, despite the class already having the tool to report one
    - **Where:** app/Site/Pools/PoolResolver.php:488-499 (iso())
    - **Affects:** Public sitepage/CDN-cached pool payloads using `publishedAt`, `firstSeenAt`, `startsAt`, or `reviewedAt`; a malformed date silently becomes `null` on the public payload.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - `PoolResolver` already `use`s `EscalatesRepeatedFaults` (`app/Site/Pools/PoolResolver.php:7,48`) and calls `self::escalateIfSustained($e, '<key>')` elsewhere in the same class (line 883, see the dropped `pools.ingest_badges_read_failed` finding below, which is correctly instrumented). Reuse the identical pattern here: `Log::warning(...)` + `self::escalateIfSustained($e, 'pool_iso_parse')` inside the catch, instead of a bare `return null`.
    - **Technical:** Category 1/4. `Carbon::parse()` is intentionally defensive because manual overrides can carry unparseable strings — the docblock explains this. But the `catch (\Throwable)` block has zero logging and zero escalation, unlike the sibling `ingest.sources` catch 380 lines later in the same file, which uses exactly the machinery this method is missing. Since this runs on a CDN-cacheable public payload path, a bad date can be published as "date missing" indefinitely with no way for triage to distinguish "legitimately absent" from "silently discarded."
    - **Plain English:** If a date in the content library has a typo or an unexpected format, the public page just leaves the date off. Nobody is alerted, and support can't tell whether the date was intentionally absent or silently dropped — even though the file has the exact alarm wired up for a very similar case a few hundred lines away.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        try {
            return Carbon::parse((string) $value)->utc()->toIso8601String();
        } catch (\Throwable) {
            return null;
        }
        ```

## P3 — Nice to have

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-17** · P3 — R2 orphan cleanup failure after image processing is warning-only
    - **Where:** app/Jobs/ProcessImageVariantsJob.php:323-333 (cleanupR2Artifacts)
    - **Affects:** Media storage hygiene; orphaned R2 artifacts can accumulate without alerting.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add `report($e)` in `cleanupR2Artifacts()`'s catch block. The job has already terminally failed and reported by the time this runs (`failed()` calls `report($e)` first), so this is purely about surfacing the *secondary* cleanup failure, not double-reporting the primary one.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. `failed()` calls `cleanupR2Artifacts()`, which catches `Throwable` and only emits `Log::warning`. A cleanup failure after the primary job has already failed is invisible unless someone reads Cloud logs directly, and can lead to unbounded orphan accumulation on R2 over time. Low severity: no user-facing correctness impact, purely storage hygiene.
    - **Plain English:** After a photo-processing failure, the system tries to clear away the half-finished files. If that cleanup also fails, it leaves the mess behind and only writes a note nobody watches.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            Log::warning('ProcessImageVariantsJob: R2 orphan cleanup failed.', [
                'image_id' => $this->imageId,
                'error' => $e->getMessage(),
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-18** · P3 — `FreshaStaffMatcher` swallows every `Throwable` from the optional Instagram enrichment lookup with no breadcrumb
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/FreshaStaffMatcher.php (matchWithTier, Instagram enrichment catch)
    - **Affects:** Fresha auto-selection confidence — a broken optional signal silently degrades the vanity-name match tier.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Add a `Log::debug` or gated `Log::warning` when the optional enrichment read fails, so a broken signal is at least discoverable during triage. Given this is genuinely optional and documented as "must never break matching," a full report()/alert is not warranted — this is a hygiene-tier gap, not a silent-failure risk to core functionality.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. The catch suppresses every `Throwable` — including real read faults, not just the documented "missing table (unit-test mirror)" case — without any log line. The comment correctly identifies this as intentionally non-fatal optional enrichment; the only gap is that a genuine, ongoing read fault is completely undiagnosable without adding instrumentation.
    - **Plain English:** A matchmaker checks an optional tip from a user's Instagram to be more confident about who a Fresha listing belongs to. If that check breaks, it silently proceeds without it — we'd have no way to know the helpful signal went dark.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        } catch (\Throwable) {
            // Optional enrichment only — a missing table (unit-test mirror) or
            // any read fault must never break matching.
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-19** · P3 — Instagram video-mirror drop reasons are logged at `info`, obscuring degraded-media outcomes during triage
    - **Where:** app/Services/Platforms/InstagramConnectionSeeder.php:536-542 (logVideoMirrorDrop)
    - **Affects:** Log triage — failed reel fetches (`bad_status_403`, `oversize_actual`, `connection_exception`, `retries_exhausted`) look like routine info, easy to filter out during incident review.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Change `logVideoMirrorDrop` to `Log::warning` while keeping the existing PII-safe host-only context (`PiiLogHygieneSweepTest` already covers that convention and needs no changes).
    - **Technical:** Category 4. `logVideoMirrorDrop` is invoked for user-visible degraded outcomes (reel fails to mirror, falls back to poster), not for routine lifecycle events, so `info` under-represents severity and can be filtered out of a triage sweep by log level.
    - **Plain English:** When a video can't be saved, the system writes a note but labels it "FYI" instead of "needs attention." That makes it easy to miss during an incident review.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        private function logVideoMirrorDrop(string $reason, string $url): void
        {
            Log::info('instagram.mirror_video.dropped', [
                'reason' => $reason,
                'host' => parse_url($url, PHP_URL_HOST),
            ]);
        }
        ```

- [ ] **#W2-OBS-20** · P3 — `SocialActorDriver` records every event, including failures, at `info` level
    - **Where:** app/Ingest/Runtime/Effects/SocialActorDriver.php:164-174 (log)
    - **Affects:** Cloud log triage for the TikTok/Facebook ingest pipeline.
    - **Effort:** S (~0.5–1h)
    - **What to do:**
        - Split `log()` by outcome: keep `social.actor.ok` at `info`, move `social.actor.threw` / `social.actor.not_ok` / `social.actor.no_payload` to `warning`.
    - **Technical:** Category 4. The single `log()` helper hardcodes `Log::info` for every event including exception paths and non-2xx responses. Production `LOG_LEVEL` defaults to `warning`, so these failure records can be silently dropped depending on config, and even when retained, severity mismatch misleads triage — failed scrapes read identically to routine successes in a log stream.
    - **Plain English:** The system writes "everything is fine" notes even when the social-media connector just failed. When the team searches logs after an outage, the important clues are either missing (if the log level filters them) or buried under routine chatter.
    - **Evidence:**
        ```php
        private function log(string $event, string $name, BilledEffectContext $ctx, array $extra): void
        {
            // info level: cloud env:logs surfaces info, and a failed scrape must
            // be diagnosable from the stream.
            Log::info($event, $extra + [
                'actor_name' => $name,
                'source_id' => $ctx->sourceId,
                'run_id' => $ctx->runId,
                'user_id' => $ctx->userId,
            ]);
        }
        ```

## Suggested Bundled Sessions

- **Bundle 1 — Moderation enforcement job completion integrity:** #OBS-1, #OBS-2
    - **Why grouped:** Same file family (`app/Jobs/Moderation/*`), same root cause (action-log entry marked `completed` without checking whether the underlying enforcement write actually happened), same fix shape (`$this->fail()` via the shared `HasActionLogLifecycle` trait).
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 2 — Content popularity scoring command observability:** #OBS-6, #OBS-7, #OBS-8
    - **Why grouped:** All three live in `ComputeContentPopularityScores.php` and share the same theme — internal catches that never reach the schedule's existing `onFailure` reporting hook.
    - **Model:** Plan: Opus · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 3 — Scraper exception/log-level hygiene (Instagram + Fresha):** #OBS-11, #OBS-12, #OBS-18, #OBS-19
    - **Why grouped:** Same subsystem (`app/Services/Platforms/*` scrapers), same pattern (inconsistent report()/log-level across near-identical catch blocks within each file).
    - **Model:** Plan: Sonnet · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 4 — YouTube scraper Nightwatch reporting:** #OBS-4, #OBS-13
    - **Why grouped:** Both in `YoutubeScraper.php`, same fix shape (add `report()` to existing catch blocks without changing fallback behaviour).
    - **Model:** Plan: Sonnet · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 5 — ProjectionWriter cap-hit reporting:** #OBS-14, #OBS-15
    - **Why grouped:** Same file, same author-acknowledged "silent cap is a defect" pattern, same fix (add `report()` beside the existing structured `Log::warning`).
    - **Model:** Plan: Sonnet · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

- **Bundle 6 — SocialActorDriver alerting + log level:** #OBS-5, #OBS-20
    - **Why grouped:** Same file, same class, touched together to avoid a second pass over the same ~40-line method.
    - **Model:** Plan: Sonnet · Implement: Sonnet · Review: Sonnet.

## Standalone — do NOT bundle

- **#OBS-3 — MediaMirror image `put()` ignored:** P1 data-integrity bug affecting what the system records as successfully mirrored; isolated file, best reviewed alone against the working video-branch pattern it needs to mirror.
- **#OBS-9 — GoogleBusinessEnrichJob soft-failure not reported:** isolated file with substantial pre-existing lock/cache/idempotency complexity (JOB-2 cost-guard markers) — bundling risks a reviewer missing an interaction with that logic.
- **#OBS-10 — GeneratePreAccountSiteJob missing-user/site not reported:** isolated file, unrelated subsystem to the other bundles.
- **#OBS-16 — PoolResolver::iso() swallows parse failures:** touches the public sitepage payload path; isolated fix, reviewed alone against the file's own `EscalatesRepeatedFaults` precedent.
- **#OBS-17 — R2 orphan cleanup warning-only:** P3, isolated file, no natural bundle partner; small enough to also qualify as an opportunistic fix if `ProcessImageVariantsJob.php` is next opened for unrelated work.
